# Rebind - Development Plan and Verification Log

**Plan status:** Core keyboard/mouse shortcut remapping and Phase 2 wheel, double/long press, four cardinal pointer gestures, capture lifecycle and ordinary opt-in mouse diagnostics are implemented. On 2026-10-08 the user withdrew HID inventory/capability reporting and physical-device attribution; their code, UI, private APIs and 21 tests were removed. Phase 3 now implements simple manual profiles: independent rule sets, one selection for all categories, CRUD/copy, Menu Bar/main-window switching and schema-6 migration from 1–5. **479 tests in 63 suites** pass (476 retained + 3 click/idle-frame recovery functions). Phase 4 now adds a shared searchable keyboard/audio/window action catalog, typed dispatch and public Core Audio volume up/down/mute with runtime capability checks; the user reports the configured audio mapping and existing bindings work after this change. Exact actions/triggers were not restated; output replacement, all audio variants and cancellation/fault regressions still need hardware checks. Phase 4 also implements public AX close/minimize/full-screen-button/hide-active-app actions with target/focus checks, bounded IPC requests and cancellation before mutation; the user reports all four actions work after manual testing. Specific target apps, trigger chords and full-screen entry/exit details were not restated; cancellation/fault/latency checks remain open. Phase 4 now also implements opening/activating a selected app and opening HTTP/HTTPS addresses or local files/folders through public NSWorkspace, native parameter pickers and one bounded async handoff. Software and Russian picker/validation checks pass; the user reports the requested opening/activation actions work. Exact apps, targets, triggers and closed-app behavior were not restated; cancellation/fault/latency checks remain open. Apple Shortcuts now has explicit UUID-based selection, native CLI execution on the serial worker, cancellation and bounded waiting; software tests, native listing and the Russian picker pass, and the user confirms requested command execution works; detailed rename/fault/cancellation gates remain open. The user confirms direct Mission Control, App Exposé and Show Desktop work, while both keyboard Space-navigation attempts fail with unintended Control/Option-like effects. Windows keyboard Command/Option remapping is confirmed in read-only settings; Control remains unremapped in those records and the saved previous-Space rule correctly stores Control+Left. Delivery cause remains unproven. The user reports the subsequent instant Dock swipe does move Spaces but fails acceptance: abrupt transition and black-screen bounce past the final desktop. That backend/private IOHID serialization is removed. Previous/next Space now sends the standard Control+arrow pair with secondary-Fn through public Quartz HID-state source and HID posting, checked against pinned MIT spacekit. This differs from both failed session-posting attempts. The user now reports everything works well after the requested native-animation/both-direction/first-and-last-desktop retest; individual cases and timings were not restated. This confirms the reported happy path, not all Reduce Motion/display/modifier/lifecycle combinations. Disabled/custom/malformed symbolic-hotkey overrides prevent keyboard posting with a localized error; settings are never changed. Public API support removes the private-layout 26.6/27 gate; app deployment remains 26. Schema 20 adds public-AX next/previous window cycling within the active application and reads 1–19 without rewriting on load. A bounded worker-owned order survives AX reordering, excludes nonstandard/minimized/modal/foreign windows, resets on membership/app/generation changes and verifies focus after Main/raise. The user confirms the requested window-cycle happy path (2026-10-09); individual app/trigger/focus cases were not restated. Schema 21 adds Previous Application with two observed running-app handles in memory; schemas 1–20 migrate without rewriting on load. The user confirms switching from Rebind works but Messages ↔ Finder does not. Public AppKit observation is retained; activation now uses off-main public AXFrontmost with actual focus readback instead of the cooperative AppKit request. The user confirms the AX fix works (2026-10-09), following the requested background-app retest; exact individual apps/triggers were not restated. Broader safety/platform checks remain open. Schema-19 experimental screen locking and schema-18 public IOKit system sleep are retained. Lock uses a dynamically resolved login-framework void entry point on reviewed macOS 26/27 majors, never a keyboard fallback; unavailable rules pass through. Native console/lock checks and lock notifications suspend recording/capture/diagnostics with independent sleep/inactive-user guards. The user confirms the requested lock → unlock → existing-bindings check works (2026-10-09); exact trigger, OS and individual cases were not restated. Broader lifecycle/latency/platform checks remain open. System sleep and selected-app Quit/System Settings/window layouts/media/mouse output are retained. Sleep runs on the serial worker with owned connection cleanup, final cancellation and accepted-request queue invalidation; the user reports the requested sleep/wake and existing-bindings check works (2026-10-09). Individual cases, exact trigger and source-release behavior were not restated; detailed safety/platform checks remain open. Quit and System Settings use public AppKit and the existing bounded Workspace path; Quit requires a unique running regular selected-copy instance and reports request acceptance only. The user confirms these requested actions work (2026-10-09); exact app/trigger/save-dialog/background cases were not separately restated, so broader guards remain open. Media events are complete tagged plain auxiliary pairs through public AppKit/Quartz HID posting on the serial worker, independent of Core Audio volume capabilities; player selection/response belongs to macOS; the user confirms delivery from mouse/additional buttons in multiple applications and background/minimized contexts (2026-10-09). Exact applications/triggers and detailed guard cases were not restated. Four public-AX layout presets now place an ordinary focused window on its current display: left/right half, fill usable area and center preserving size. Geometry, cancellation and persistence are software-tested; the user confirms left/right halves, maximizing and minimizing in multiple applications (2026-10-09). Exact app names, rules and maximize/full-screen distinction were not restated; centering and detailed multi-display/safety checks remain open. Mouse batches are balanced/tagged/plain, with final cancellation checks and a held-target guard; the user confirms all four click outputs work, including in third-party apps, without naming individual apps/triggers. Detailed modifier/button/pointer/cancellation gates remain open. Earlier desktop migrations still convert legacy actions across profiles and retire their old output chords while preserving all other fields/actions. Previously inspected native Russian UI and current Debug/universal Release checks pass. The user confirms that switching profiles applies the selected profile’s own bindings; held-source cancellation, relaunch and the specific menu/window selection paths remain manual gates. Previously confirmed G603 LIGHTSPEED/G HUB ordinary, 1000 ms hold, leftward gesture, Pause/Resume, sleep/wake and basic side-button diagnostics remain evidence for those existing paths, not all cancellation/fault cases. Complex pointer shapes and automatic device/profile activation are out of scope. Phase 5 has a pinned, separately built/tested Subsurface contact probe and confirmed built-in hardware / tap and click 3/4 scope. Availability succeeds; the adapter labels the default device `other` despite `built_in=true`. The initial unattended check got zero frames; the user-run 30-second retest supplied 1010 frames with contacts 0–4 and no rejected frames or reported frame-ID discontinuities. Raw built-in acquisition is user-verified; probe-only short-contact candidate recognition is implemented/software-tested and the user transcript matches three light taps each for 3/4 fingers. The user-run passive button/contact mode receives 18 button records without overflow; first three pairs have a recent consumed zero-contact count, so tap/click separation remains ambiguous. Shared relative receipt timing is now user-verified: a 3-finger candidate is followed by left down/up at +12/+28 ms with zero nearby contacts; the 4-finger candidate has no button pair in that run. This is not physical-source or causal proof. An explicit standalone one-shot four-finger Command-W check is now user-verified after the empty-tail correction: one 176 ms candidate, one posting attempt, shortcut-posted exit and reported successful effect. This verifies the built-in device happy path in the separate tool, not Rebind gesture rules, physical-source attribution, suppression or lifecycle safety. The production target now integrates an experimental built-in four-finger single tap → configurable keyboard shortcut, a bounded private contact adapter, normal profiles/schema 22 and cancellation-aware targeted dispatch. Three-finger/physical-click/double/corner mappings, the full shared action catalog for touch, and external trackpads remain unavailable. The user confirms integrated app delivery (2026-10-10), but reports roughly 1/7 recognition and noticeable latency versus BetterTouchTool. The responsiveness fix preserves native callback timing for empty release arrays, broadens contact tolerances and reduces the settling check from 500 to 60 ms. The user then reports improved initial detection followed by no tap output after four-finger clicks. The click-to-tap synthetic regression exposed an idle-forward-frame-gap cancellation loop; the fix allows forward gaps only between known-neutral strokes, retaining active/pending-output discontinuity guards. Actual failure cause, revised recovery/latency, tap/click exclusivity, adapter stop/recovery and system conflicts remain hardware gates; suppression is not offered. Remaining core hardware safety checks and touch feasibility stay open.
**Last reviewed:** 2026-10-10 (Previous Application AX happy path user-confirmed; Phase 5 pinned library audit and standalone contact-acquisition probe built/tested; built-in raw contact acquisition user-verified; short-contact light-tap subset user-verified; passive button delivery user-verified; shared receipt timing user-verified; pure tap sequencing/MIT corner regions and standalone four-finger Command-W delivery user-confirmed; experimental app four-finger rules integrated/software-tested; app happy-path delivery user-confirmed but recognition/latency failed usability; responsiveness and click-to-tap idle-frame recovery fixes software-tested, revised hardware/click/lifecycle checks and T1 exit pending).
**Owner:** Coding agent, with user decisions on major architecture or license trade-offs.  
**Product requirements:** `SPEC.md`  
**Engineering rules:** `../AGENTS.md`

## How to use this file

- Work in **thin, verifiable vertical slices**. Do not build months of UI ahead of the functional input engine.
- Before changing a box to `[x]`, record concrete evidence: files changed, build/test commands and results, actual macOS/device test when needed. `[x]` means the stated acceptance criteria are met, not merely attempted.
- `Implemented` does not equal `Verified`. Hardware-dependent features remain unverified without physical testing. Use the capability status vocabulary in `SPEC.md` §13.
- If an OS limitation prevents a task, note reproduction steps, research sources, workaround options, and product/UI implications under **Research findings / blockers**.
- Keep all phase names, outcomes, and next actions up to date for a new agent session. Add concise entries to **Session log** after substantive changes.
- Preparation checks below have concrete evidence in the session log. Product and hardware checks remain open until independently verified.

Scope: Phase 0; `SPEC.md` §§6–7, 10–12, 14. This session configures development only. None of K1/K2, M1/M2, D1/D2, T1/T2, G1/G2, R1, A1/A2, or S1/S2/S3 has passed a Rebind implementation test.

- [x] Inventory existing SwiftUI application, project/workspace, assets, Git, documentation and both references.
- [x] Verify installed full Xcode, compiler, SDK, SwiftPM, and local build destination.
- [x] Configure macOS 26.0 minimum, Swift 6 mode/complete concurrency checking, and Rebind display identity.
- [x] Disable application sandbox for intended interception/actions; retain build-script sandboxing and enable Hardened Runtime.
- [x] Preserve `blinks44.rebind` and automatic signing; verify local ad-hoc build/launch without inventing credentials.
- [x] Add hosted `rebindTests` target and shared `rebind` scheme; compile Swift Testing and app-module imports; execute empty runner successfully.
- [x] Review and install four revision-pinned project skills with licenses and checksums; verify actual files and Git visibility.
- [x] Extend `.gitignore`; verify no user-specific Xcode files are tracked, and all existing requirements/references are tracked.
- [x] Research native APIs and runtime candidates; add no speculative runtime packages.
- [x] Add reproducible `scripts/dev.sh` commands and developer `README.md`; verify Debug/Release builds, test build/run, package resolution and app launch.

### Configuration decisions

- Original project is a small Xcode 27 starter: target `rebind`, `ContentView.swift`, `rebindApp.swift`, synchronized source group, empty icon slots, no entitlements file, no packages, no test target/shared scheme. Existing source files are unchanged. Preserve project format/object version and source directories.
- Host: macOS **27.0.1 (26A434)**, Apple Silicon. Full Xcode **27.0 (27A266a)**, Apple Swift **6.4 (swiftlang-6.4.0.34.1)**, SDK **27.0 (26A425)**; `swift package --version` reports **Swift Package Manager – Swift 6.4.0-dev**. These are installed-toolchain facts, not a promise of macOS 26 runtime verification.
- `xcode-select -p` points to `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools`; bare `xcodebuild` fails. `scripts/dev.sh` respects explicit `DEVELOPER_DIR`/valid system selection, otherwise uses installed full Xcode per process. No system-wide selection or security changes.
- Both app configurations use `SWIFT_VERSION = 6.0`, `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete`, deployment **26.0** instead of 27.0. Preserve approachable concurrency, member-import visibility, and **MainActor default isolation**. Future synchronous C/CF tap types must explicitly use appropriate `nonisolated` boundaries. Test target has no MainActor default, so isolated app code requires explicit actor-aware tests.
- Product/display name **Rebind**, Swift module **rebind**, target/scheme/project **rebind**. Bundle ID **blinks44.rebind** remains unchanged. Test bundle **blinks44.rebind.tests** depends on and is hosted by the actual app; no test framework package is necessary.
- `ENABLE_APP_SANDBOX = NO`: `SPEC.md` requires filtering/synthesis and cross-app AX operations; Apple documents restrictions on assistive Accessibility and related operations in [App Sandbox](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/protecting-user-data-with-app-sandbox). Outside-Mac-App-Store distribution accepts the loss of app-container isolation; future code must minimize local access and permissions. Remove unused sandbox file-access and app-group-registration settings. No Full Disk Access, root, SIP changes, or new capabilities.
- `ENABLE_HARDENED_RUNTIME = YES`; keep `ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING = YES`. Release sets `CODE_SIGN_INJECT_BASE_ENTITLEMENTS = NO` because observed Xcode defaults otherwise injected `get-task-allow`. Final Release signature has `runtime` flag and **no entitlements**. Debug retains Xcode debugger/testing grants; Xcode disables its hardened runtime during ad-hoc debugging/testing. No manually authored entitlement file.
- No existing development team; `security find-identity -v -p codesigning` found zero usable identities. Automatic **Sign to Run Locally** uses ad-hoc signing successfully. Stable Apple Development identity is recommended before TCC/hardware work; Developer ID/notarization remains a credentials-dependent distribution task. Keep bundle ID and launch path stable; do not create certificates, reset TCC, or grant permissions as preparation.
- `rebindTests/TestSupport.swift` only imports `Testing` and `@testable import rebind`; **zero tests** is deliberate. Meaningful tests start with Phase 1. Shared scheme supports Build, Run, Test, Profile, Analyze, and Archive.
- `.gitignore` keeps `Package.resolved`, shared schemes, `.agents/skills`, docs, and references visible; ignores macOS metadata, IDE user data, DerivedData/build/SwiftPM caches, and result/archive/symbol bundles. The previously tracked scheme-management user file was already removed by the user by the final inventory; local user data remains on disk. No Git index changes, commits, pushes, remotes, hooks, or identity configuration added. Existing docs/references became tracked during this session through user activity and were preserved.
- Both references inspected: `design.png` shows the intended pane hierarchy; `interface.png` shows a macOS VM interface/material direction. References do not expand product scope to Touch Bar/Siri Remote or prove input-device availability. Icon design, real UI, and localization remain roadmap work.

## Milestones

### Phase 0 - Repository inventory, feasibility and dependency audit

**Goal:** avoid architecture mistakes before writing low-level interception code; immediately move toward a working prototype when feasibility is clear.

- [x] Inspect project target(s), source layout, bundle ID, entitlements, sandbox setting, build scheme, current macOS deployment target, Swift language mode, tests, and existing work. **Preserve the existing project.**
- [x] Confirm toolchain availability (Xcode / `xcodebuild`, build destination, Swift version). Document exact command to build the app.
- [x] Read relevant Apple docs for `CGEventTap`, event types, synthesis, IOKit/HID, Accessibility, secure input, permission prompts, background app lifecycle, and `SMAppService`.
- [ ] Confirm which combinations allow interception plus suppression, which permit observation only, and which are blocked by the OS.
- Physical-device discovery/attribution withdrawn by user decision 2026-10-08; no remaining per-device gate. Preserve numeric Quartz input and category rules.
- [ ] Investigate normal and extra buttons beyond #5, including differing HID/CGEvent numbering, firmware/driver overrides, and limits.
- [ ] Investigate real trackpad four-finger tap capture, native gesture conflicts, and suppression feasibility.
- [ ] Investigate Magic Mouse contact capture separately from trackpad. Do not assume one library covers both.
- [x] Compare reusable packages/references with license, Swift/macOS compatibility, sandbox requirements and maintenance status. Review code/skill content before installing.
- [ ] Decide smallest architecture and confirm which feature claims are `Core`, `Experimental`, `Blocked`, or `Later`.
- [ ] Record design decisions and risk matrix below; update this plan if a genuine platform constraint is discovered.

**Exit evidence:** written findings plus working build command, justified capture approach, explicit multitouch/device-identification risks. Start Phase 1 directly when enough is understood; avoid endless research with no tested prototype.

### Phase 1 - Smallest working global remapping slice

**Goal:** prove the app can recognize, match, suppress when allowed, and dispatch an action in a separate foreground app.

**Current scope:** `SPEC.md` §§2, 3.1–3.2, 4, 5 (shortcut output), 6–9, 11–12; K1/K2, M1/M2, R1, A2 and S1/S2/S3. Implementation is category-wide, ordinary physical keys or middle/extra mouse buttons, exact four primary modifiers, one shortcut pulse per press. [Verification and pending physical walkthrough](verification/phase-1.md).

- [x] Define stable `Binding`, `DeviceScope`, `InputTrigger`, `ModifierSet`, `RebindAction`, and useful diagnostics types (`Codable`, testable). Version-1 configuration validation and round-trip tests pass; unsupported scope/key/action and conflicting active rules are rejected.
- [x] Implement deterministic matching for one physical keyboard trigger and for a normal extra mouse button plus exact modifiers. Pure tests verify exact match/nomatch, unlimited numeric button identifiers, disabled/conflicting rules, and press/release ownership. The user verified Control + G603 Button 4 versus plain Button 4; keyboard and other modifier sets remain pending.
- [ ] Create a real global input capture path without UI dependency. **Implemented:** public active session `CGEventTap` on a dedicated CF run loop; user-confirmed G603 middle/side recording proves mouse capture now runs after permission setup. The user also confirmed Control + Button 4 → Command+A in TextEdit with plain Button 4 unaffected. Physical keyboard and lifecycle checks remain pending.
- [x] Separate the real-time tap callback from action execution; ensure passthrough for unmatched events. Inspected synchronous callback; Mutex protects brief metadata operations, queue capped at 16; worker dispatches between callbacks. Overflow, editing/pause, synthetic feedback and unmatched-event policies have tests. Actual event-path performance remains unmeasured.
- [ ] Implement synthesized shortcut sending with correct down/up lifecycle and protection against recursive processing. **Implemented:** private source, explicit output flags, balanced tagged key pair; construction tests pass without posting. No persistent synthetic held states are created. User-verified Control-held mouse source → Command+A in TextEdit works; other recipients/flag combinations and held-output behavior remain unverified; output needing modifier transitions/held output is deferred.

The agent observed that saved row after schema-4 loading. Other editor actions and keyboard capture remain pending.

Pure pause/cancellation/emergency tests pass; real held-input and menu-bar execution need manual checks. Closing the window was observed to leave the same process alive.

**Exit evidence:** the minimum external remap is now user-confirmed: G603 LIGHTSPEED + G HUB, Control + Button 4 → Command+A in TextEdit, plain Button 4 unchanged. This closes the first end-to-end evidence gate; full Phase 1 keyboard, suppression/release and lifecycle boxes remain open. Continue Phase 2 while verifying these remaining paths.

### Phase 2 - Robust keyboard and mouse engine

**Goal:** production-quality core capture and matching, not just a proof-of-concept.

**Implemented slices:** `SPEC.md` §§2, 3.1–3.2, 4, 5 (shortcut output), 6, 9, 12; K1/K2, M1/M2, S1/S2/S3.
Category-wide discrete vertical/horizontal wheel with exact primary modifiers,
one pulse per eligible event, shared 100 ms limiter. Continuous/momentum/diagonal
scroll passes through. [Implemented limits and pending walkthrough](verification/phase-2-wheel.md).
Keys/middle-extra buttons can also use exclusive double-press activation with the
system double-click interval, release requirement, cancellation and no delayed
single fallback. [Double-press limits and walkthrough](verification/phase-2-double-press.md).
The ordinary G603 remap and subsequent Button 5 long press, no modifiers, 1000 ms → Command+A have user-confirmed external results. The initial hold attempt failed; after owned-mouse-motion and UI-focus cancellation fixes, the user reports that all configured combinations and the delay work. This confirms the configured mouse happy path, not the full hardware cancellation/suppression matrix or measured timing precision. Long press sends one pulse after a per-rule 100–5000 ms threshold, default 500 ms; short presses are consumed. [Contract, failure/fix and verification](verification/phase-2-hold.md).

### Phase 5 - Multitouch trackpad feasibility and implementation

**User scope confirmed — 2026-10-09:** built-in trackpad available; both single tap and physical click with 3/4 fingers are explicitly required. Retain the earlier compact double tap 2/3/4, physical click 2/3/4 and one-finger four-corner set. SPEC §3.4 is narrowed accordingly: general one/two-finger taps, five-finger gestures, holds/swipes/pinch/rotation/Force Click are out of scope. Native scrolling/navigation stays active; this does not redefine Magic Mouse capabilities. Confirmation establishes desired behavior, not hardware support.

- Candidate shortlist rechecked: [OpenMultitouchSupport](https://github.com/Kyome22/OpenMultitouchSupport) (MIT, default trackpad only, private framework; manifest tools 6.2/macOS 15, binary 4.0.0 + AsyncAlgorithms), [Subsurface](https://github.com/mrkai77/Subsurface) (Apache-2.0, raw contacts/multiple-trackpad monitoring; manifest tools 6.2/macOS 13, Scribe on mutable main), and [Sanne](https://github.com/anandghegde/sanne) as an application reference. Prior license/maintenance audits remain in the dependency register; this recheck inspected READMEs/manifests, not current commit dates, local builds or callback correctness. These are historical shortlist notes; the 2026-10-09 pinned source audit and isolated Subsurface probe below supersede the no-install status.
- Apple's [gesture guide](https://support.apple.com/en-us/102482) confirms that two-finger click/tap overlaps Secondary Click and two-finger double tap overlaps Smart Zoom; corner taps may overlap ordinary tap-to-click. System settings allow changing/disabling some gestures, so “system-reserved” does not establish universal impossibility. Raw contacts do not prove suppression. Check native conflicts and physical-click/contact timing on actual hardware before advertising replacement; never silently change system settings.

- [x] User-run built-in raw contact acquisition (2026-10-09): 30 seconds, 1010 frames, counts 0–4, states 1–7, no rejected frames, no reported frame-ID discontinuities, peak pressure 118. Default kind other / built_in true; exact Mac model/OS not restated, current host inventory macOS 27.0.1. Acquisition only, not gesture/suppression validation.
- [ ] Verify Magic Trackpad acquisition and native adapter teardown/recovery/protected-session behavior on actual hardware.
- [x] Implement/test probe-only bounded short-contact candidates for 3/4 fingers: one-shot end, per-contact movement/identity, rolling arrival/lift, malformed metadata/gaps/time/cancel guards. Not tap/click classification or production rules.
- [x] User transcript matches three 3-finger and three 4-finger short-contact candidates in the requested light-tap check (2026-10-09); duration 128–152 ms. Final report/exact negative cases were not supplied.
- [ ] Complete deliberate hold/movement negatives and independent click exclusion before using candidates for tap actions.
- [x] User-run combined probe receives passive button down/up metadata: installed observer, 18 records / nine pairs, no overflow, time-limit stop (2026-10-09). This verifies delivery for this run only, not physical source, native teardown/recovery or click separation.
- [ ] Hardware-verify consumed contact-count freshness, shared receipt timing/order, other-device and native tap-to-click ambiguity. The first three reported pairs occur with a recent consumed zero-contact count; do not equate them with physical clicks. No physical source or suppression claim.
- [x] Audit pinned OpenMultitouchSupport/Subsurface sources, callback/buffering/logging behavior, licenses and manifests; compare public AppKit. Build/test an isolated Subsurface feasibility probe with all dependencies locked. See research below.
- [x] Implement/software-test experimental built-in four-finger single-tap rules, schema 22 migration, native preset editor, bounded private adapter and existing-worker targeted shortcut dispatch. No runtime package added; [contract/manual checks](verification/phase-5-four-finger-tap.md).
- [ ] Approve the experimental adapter for production after actual app delivery, physical-click timing, callback stop/recovery and system-gesture conflicts are verified. Current private ABI/retained process-lifetime context decision is below.
- [ ] Evaluate selective reuse of Sanne's MIT recognizers and investigate WheelClick's claimed public-API trackpad path before expanding custom recognition; see the tap/click alternatives review below. Neither establishes physical-click separation on our hardware.
- [x] Adapt/test Sanne's MIT four-corner region function, retaining attribution; no capture engine, logging, locks, UI or action dispatch imported.
- [x] Implement/test pure qualified-tap sequencing: configured single 3/4 versus double 2/3/4, corner singles, no pair reuse, deadlines, cancellation and unknown-click exclusion. No global adapter or rule dispatch wired; hardware gates stay open.
- [ ] Build single-tap classification for 3/4 fingers, double taps for 2/3/4 and one-finger corner taps; test movement, noise, contact-count transitions, gaps, cancellations and timing.
- [ ] Establish physical-click/contact correlation for 2/3/4 fingers; pressure alone is insufficient. Keep tap and click exclusive, with one-shot delivery.
- [ ] Add suitable modifier+gesture combinations when modifier state is available during recognition.
- Pinch/rotation/Force Click, five-finger gestures and holds/swipes are outside the confirmed trackpad scope; do not implement them as Phase 5 stretch features.
- [ ] Test system gesture conflicts and suppression limitations in System Settings permutations.
- [x] Build/test an explicit standalone one-shot four-finger Command-W mode with delayed button exclusion and cancellation; not integrated into Rebind.
- [x] User confirms standalone built-in four-finger tap → Command-W happy path (2026-10-09): one candidate, one posted pair, shortcut-posted stop. Broader safety/system-conflict and production acceptance remain open.
- [x] User confirms integrated Rebind four-finger tap delivery (2026-10-10), but reports roughly 1 successful tap in 7 and noticeable delay. This verifies output only; usability and T1/T2 are not accepted.
- [ ] Recheck integrated recognition/latency after the empty-callback/tolerance/60 ms settling and idle-frame recovery fixes, including click → tap recovery, quick physical-click negatives and double effects. T1/T2, exclusivity and lifecycle remain hardware gates.

- [x] Add recognition pure-logic tests for the integrated four-finger subset (contact sequences, one-shot firing, reset/cancel), separate from hardware tests. Broader gesture gates remain open.

**Exit evidence:** a truthful trackpad capability matrix and hardware-verified mapping, or a documented blocker with a safe user-facing limitation.

### Phase 6 - Magic Mouse integration

- [ ] Inspect hardware and current low-level frameworks for genuine Magic Mouse touch events.

- [ ] Reuse trackpad recognition when semantics match, without forcing mismatched hardware into identical gestures.
- [ ] Add capture/recording and action binding for verified supported gestures.
- [ ] Test actual Magic Mouse gestures, native scrolling conflicts, disconnect/reconnect, and pause.

**Exit evidence:** supported Magic Mouse gestures are verified on named real hardware; other requested gestures are clearly labeled as unsupported/experimental.

### Phase 7 - Settings, persistence, permissions and background lifecycle

- [ ] Versioned, atomic local JSON configuration; schema evolution, corrupt file recovery, import/export/reset.
- [ ] App window can close while engine and Menu Bar menu remain active.
- [ ] Launch at Login using ServiceManagement (`SMAppService`) and real state checks.
- [ ] First-run permissions onboarding; accurate Accessibility/Input Monitoring state, action-specific Automation requests.
- [ ] Revalidation and clear guidance when returning from System Settings; helpful errors for tap creation failure.
- [ ] Settings: General, Permissions, Input, Language, Advanced; About screen with version/licenses.
- [ ] Ensure repeated window opening does not create duplicate taps/services.
- [ ] Verify relaunch, login, sleep/wake, permission revocation, and paused/disabled behavior.

**Exit evidence:** reliable long-running background utility with recoverable persisted state and no fake permission toggles.

### Phase 8 - Full SwiftUI experience and visual polish

- [x] Create a separate optical Menu Bar template from the live Sketch brand mark, integrate the vector PDF, and verify Debug/Release native resource loading (18 pt, template rendering), 2026-10-10. Live menu appearance after relaunch remains unverified.
- [x] Design first-launch/permissions states in live Sketch: Accessibility missing in Light/Dark, event-posting mismatch, permissions ready, revoked access, and German text expansion; screenshot and saved-document verification. See `docs/verification/menu-bar-onboarding.md`. Native onboarding implementation and runtime accessibility/localization checks remain open.
- [x] Inspect the current Rebind design in Sketch via MCP: relevant frames, symbols/styles, layout and screenshots. All 18 current boards reviewed and identified system defects corrected; see `docs/verification/sketch-design-system-review.md` (2026-10-09). Optional `docs/references/` assets/exports are supplementary; `design.png` is no longer required.
- [ ] Implement native macOS 26 `NavigationSplitView` with devices left, bindings/actions main, divider, Settings/About at bottom.
- [ ] Binding list, searchable action chooser, recording sheets, states/conflicts/empty screens.
- [ ] Thoughtful Liquid Glass, consistent typography, spacing, platform-native feedback, SF Symbols and window resizing.
- [ ] Adapt focus/keyboard navigation; VoiceOver labels; light/dark, Reduce Motion, increased contrast.
- [x] Integrate the user-supplied provisional PNG app icon from `docs/references/Rebind-AppIcon.appiconset`; verify macOS size slots and Debug/Release asset compilation/resources.
- [x] User confirms the trial icon now displays correctly after the targeted Xcode-copy registration refresh (2026-10-09). Final artwork, small sizes/backgrounds and appearances remain open.
- [x] Integrate refined Sketch artwork as native `rebind/AppIcon.icon`; verify Debug/Release compilation, generated appearances/small-size exports, compiled ICNS and actual Finder preview on the development host (2026-10-10). See `docs/verification/app-icon-composer.md`.
- [ ] Finalize icon runtime acceptance: relaunched app's Dock, native appearances on macOS 26, and user-selected tints. Sketch contrast/material fixes and native delivery completed; no minimum-OS or live Dock claim yet.
- [ ] Manual usability pass: creating a new binding requires no raw key/button codes and as few decisions as possible.

**Exit evidence:** aesthetically finished **functional** native UI that aligns with provided references; no inert controls or placeholder data.

### Phase 9 - Four-language localization

- [ ] Add String Catalog keys and localized text for English, Russian, German, French.
- [ ] Select system default + offer manual language setting; implement genuine switching behavior or accurately explain relaunch.
- [ ] Localize actions, trigger names, errors, permission/onboarding text, Settings, About, tooltips and accessibility labels.
- [ ] Test EN/RU/DE/FR layouts, long strings, keyboard glyphs, empty states and error screens.

**Exit evidence:** core UX has no untranslated strings in any of the four selected languages.

### Phase 10 - Final integration, packaging, and reliability pass

- [ ] Re-run acceptance walkthroughs from `SPEC.md` §15; record pass/fail/experimental, macOS build and devices for each.
- [ ] Run unit tests and full Xcode build; fix issues introduced by concurrency, code signing, packaging or permissions.
- [ ] Smoke-test background operation over repeated enable/disable and sleep/wake cycles, high-frequency input and reconnects.
- [ ] Audit dependencies/licenses and produce third-party credits.
- [ ] Prepare runnable app distribution guidance for outside Mac App Store, including signing/notarization considerations if distribution is desired.
- [ ] Write concise launch/build instructions in repository documentation (may add README later).
- [ ] Conduct a final check that no experimental or missing feature is described as complete.

**Exit evidence:** reproducible build, credible hardware verification record, clean UI, safe runtime, and an honest known-limitations section.

## Research findings / blockers

### Phase 4 previous-application switching — 2026-10-09

**Current backend correction:** the user confirms Rebind → app but reports Messages ↔ Finder fails after the notification fix. The original cooperative NSRunningApplication request below is superseded for Previous Application by public `AXFrontmost` on owned background AX handles, with focus readback. AppKit history remains on MainActor; the existing 450 ms AX budget/per-call ≤150 ms and single Workspace slot are reused. The user now confirms the requested AX retest works (2026-10-09); detailed safety/platform checks remain open. See the latest session entry. The separate selected-app activation action is unchanged.

- Scope: SPEC §5 Windows/applications / A1/A2, §§2, 6–7, 9–12. Apple [activation notifications](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/didActivateApplicationNotification), [running applications](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsrunningapplication), [cooperative activation](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2023/10054/) and SDK 27 `NSRunningApplication.h` establish the public APIs and request/actual-effect distinction. Direct activation DocC pages returned JavaScript shells; installed header declarations/build supply API evidence. `activate(from:options:)` requires cooperation from the other app, which Rebind cannot arrange for arbitrary apps. Use public `activate(options: [])`, as already used for selected-app activation; deprecated ignoring-other-apps and synthetic Command-Tab are unnecessary.
- GitHub/SPI research found AppRuntimeKit: source/full MIT license/manifest read at the pinned revision in the dependency register. It provides MainActor public AppKit activation and lifecycle streams, but no previous-app product policy. Rebind already owns the bounded Workspace dispatch/lifecycle; retaining a two-handle observation state is smaller than adding a general runtime package. DockDoor and AltTab are maintained full GPL applications, not drop-in packages; source copying/integration would need a separate license decision. No upstream code copied, library installed or external app built/run.
- Observe eligible regular external frontmost apps with the Workspace notification center, MainActor selector lifecycle tied to AppModel start/stop, seeded with current foreground. Keep only two native handles, no titles/windows/process list/disk/log. Repeated observation does not lose history; requests never optimistically alter it. Resample frontmost before dispatch to reconcile delayed notification. From Rebind, return to the last external app; otherwise return to the preceding observed app. Retained terminated instances are refused, without PID lookup/relaunch/older-app fallback. Clear history on stop; no persistent usage history or system Command-Tab ordering guarantee.
- Activation is one native cooperative request with no async waiting/retry; no successful-focus claim. macOS owns hidden/minimized windows, Spaces and fullscreen effects. Profile/pause/edit/record/stop/session/Secure Input/post-permission gates reuse bounded Workspace serialization and are checked immediately before the request. Consumed input cannot be replayed safely on native failure; show a localized error. [Contract and manual checks](verification/phase-4-application-switch.md).

### Phase 5 pinned contact-acquisition research — 2026-10-09

- Public [AppKit touch handling](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Cocoa/Conceptual/EventOverview/HandlingTouchEvents/HandlingTouchEvents.html) routes a contact sequence to the view under the pointer; it does not establish global background contacts. A narrow private-framework feasibility check is justified before promising gesture replacement. GitHub sources, manifests, full licenses and maintenance metadata were retrieved; Swift Package Index searches did not establish candidate build compatibility.
- [OpenMultitouchSupport](https://github.com/Kyome22/OpenMultitouchSupport/tree/15c6bb0c6a2d2858559493a28ab23f7ac58648a3): pinned `15c6bb0`, MIT (Takuto Nakamura), tools 6.2/macOS 15+, latest commit 2026-05-28, not archived. Binary wrapper 4.0.0 and AsyncAlgorithms 1.1.4+; default-device only. Reviewed Objective-C/Swift sources expose raw-device logging, synchronous callback-queue handoff/listener lifecycle risks and latest-frame buffering; Swift touch timestamps use Date formatting rather than native frame/time metadata. Not integrated, copied or built.
- [Subsurface](https://github.com/mrkai77/Subsurface/tree/88eb16b0a6f7a44a898cecff9e283298aa413936): pinned `88eb16b`, Apache-2.0, tools 6.2/macOS 13+, latest commit 2026-09-30, not archived. Supplies native frames/states/pressure and optional dynamic symbols. Reviewed raw-count/pointer ABI, registration under locks, yielding under locks and unregister-quiescence limits remain. Direct default-device stream is latest-frame bounded; broader multi-device monitor uses an unbounded hub and is not adopted. No seize/haptics/power/palm APIs used. Private API compatibility is unguaranteed.
- **Decision:** Subsurface is used only by [TrackpadProbe](../tools/TrackpadProbe/README.md), a separate explicit ≤30-second developer acquisition tool. Rebind.app has no new package or gesture feature. [Scribe](https://github.com/SenpaiHunters/Scribe/tree/658180864653457ce201917200bac67ee3eeac87), BSD-3-Clause, tools 6/macOS 13+, latest commit 2026-01-23, is required upstream on mutable main, locked to reviewed `6581808`; its logging/macro sources were inspected and logs disabled before native calls. Macro dependency [SwiftSyntax 602.0.0](https://github.com/swiftlang/swift-syntax/tree/602.0.0), revision `4799286`, is Apache-2.0 with Runtime Library Exception. Full licenses and exact pins are retained with the tool. Installed Xcode 27/Swift 6.4 builds these versions; no macOS 26/Intel execution claim. No GPL source copied, helpers or external setup scripts.
- Report schema contains aggregate contact counts/states/pressure and discontinuities only, not coordinates/IDs/timestamps/text. No-argument/`--check` path calls availability only without device creation/start/stop. Capture checks protected session before start/each consumed frame plus a nominal 100 ms watchdog; private lock metadata and delayed polling are compatibility limits. Callback teardown/sleep/wake and native start still need hardware checks. No gesture matching, event suppression, physical-click inference or OS preference changes; native two-finger/corner conflicts remain open.

### Phase 5 experimental app adapter decision — 2026-10-09

- Purpose: the smallest app integration of the user-confirmed built-in four-finger Command-W probe. Public AppKit remains view-scoped; public Quartz supplies button/activity observation and PID-targeted output, not global contacts. Existing GitHub/Swift Package Index/native research is above. Rechecked private callback/header references and pinned Subsurface layout; no off-the-shelf embeddable component satisfying the entire safety/compact-gesture scope established. Sanne/MiddleClick/WheelClick comparisons remain useful, not a reason to import a full application.
- [Subsurface `88eb16b`](https://github.com/mrkai77/Subsurface/tree/88eb16b0a6f7a44a898cecff9e283298aa413936): Apache-2.0, tools 6.2/macOS 13+, last audited commit 2026-09-30. Its selected C signatures/96-byte contact layout inform an original narrow Rebind adapter; full Apache notice bundled. Current installed Swift 6.4/SDK 27 builds it for macOS 26 minimum. The app does **not** adopt the package, mutable-main Scribe, SwiftSyntax macros, logging, latest-frame stream, broad monitor, haptics or power APIs. No GPL code copied. Swift Package Index search did not prove an alternative's build compatibility.
- Private wrapper decisions: validate 0–16 count before any pointer read; fixed four active contacts/64-frame ring; receipt/revision/activity stamps, no native pointer escape or callback task/actor hop. Classify outside callback, conservatively cancel uncertain/stale/lost input. One native default built-in device/registration/framework handle/Sendable context is retained until process exit, avoiding unsupported unregister-quiescence assumptions and repeated pause/resume registration leaks. Stop closes acceptance before native call. Sleep/wake/private callback timing, native ABI and actual hardware delivery remain unverified in the app; a blocked MainActor delays physical stop, while mailbox gates reject protected data/output.
- Compatibility gate: macOS major 26–27 only; future releases unavailable until audited, 26/Intel runtime still unverified. Failure/unavailability is localized; start failures latch until revision/resume, native stop failure requires app restart. The first integration is unmodified single four-finger tap → **keyboard shortcut only**, preset trigger instead of fake recorder. Full shared action catalog, genuine gesture recording and other compact gestures remain required future slices; SPEC scope is retained.
- Reuse original probe thresholds, neutral-release correction and 500 ms settling with ordinary tap activity observation, then one bounded dispatch expiring after 100 ms. Targeted public CGEvent pairs use the existing serial worker; actual final monotonic expiry and cancellation are rechecked before posting. No physical-source proof, gesture suppression, accepted-effect rollback or delivery acknowledgement. UI explicitly warns that native gestures remain active and may need user settings changes. [Detailed contract/manual checks](verification/phase-5-four-finger-tap.md).

### Phase 5 tap/click alternatives review — 2026-10-09

The user asked whether additional ready-made libraries/extensions could help distinguish taps and physical clicks. The earlier contact-wrapper shortlist was insufficient to rule out existing recognition implementations. Rechecked GitHub sources, licenses, manifests and maintenance metadata, Apple touch documentation, BetterTouchTool's native trigger catalog and WheelClick's author documentation. Sources were read only; no upstream scripts, external apps, binary installs or gesture interception were run.

- [Sanne at `53bee5b`](https://github.com/anandghegde/sanne/tree/53bee5b36ebae96a8b1228221e878de7acf79149): full MIT license inspected; latest commit 2026-10-05, not archived; Swift tools 5.9, macOS 13+, no package dependencies in its manifest. Reviewed touch adapter, gesture engine, three-finger/corner recognizers and click guard. Implements three-finger tap/click, four-finger tap and corner taps, making it a stronger recognition reference than acquisition-only wrappers. Its trackpad click path observes public AppKit left-down events and asynchronously correlates them with the current contact session; its three-finger tap path checks a click flag. This is not independent physical-source proof: the click guard accepts peak count >=3, observers cannot suppress the original click, system-synthesized clicks/other mice and queue ordering remain concerns. No claim of complete 2/3/4-click/double-tap coverage, compatibility with Rebind's strict callback contracts, local build or hardware verification. Candidate for selective MIT reuse with attribution and adapted tests, not a whole-app dependency or an approved production adapter.
- [MiddleClick at `566155d`](https://github.com/artginzburg/MiddleClick/tree/566155d7e3f4f4a63d9f894ca47dc374ed070f93): GPL-3.0 full license inspected; latest commit 2026-09-02, not archived, but the maintainer declares feature development finished in favor of WheelClick. Xcode settings Swift 6/macOS 10.13; nested MoreTouch wrapper tools 5.3/macOS 10.13, private MultitouchSupport. Reviewed touch/mouse handlers: raw contact count gates left/right button down/up conversion, while duration/movement and a recent-click interval prevent some tap duplicates. Configurable finger count includes four fingers. [Upstream documents Three Finger Drag conflicts](https://github.com/artginzburg/MiddleClick/blob/566155d7e3f4f4a63d9f894ca47dc374ed070f93/docs/three-finger-drag.md), so this is not a universally reliable discriminator. Research reference only; no GPL implementation or MoreTouch source copied, and no build/runtime compatibility established here. Ventura fork `gm-vm/MiddleClick-Ventura` is also GPL-3.0, latest commit 2023-01-04; not preferable to the newer lineage.
- [Karabiner-MultitouchExtension at `ec2fea8`](https://github.com/pqrs-org/Karabiner-Elements/tree/ec2fea8940f7254a748c052a890ee9db7113d9b1/src/apps/MultitouchExtension): root Unlicense inspected; latest repository commit 2026-10-04, not archived; extension settings Swift 6/macOS 13+, private MultitouchSupport, SettingsAccess and Karabiner service/IPC integration. Reviewed its contact manager, finger state/count and C++ variable handoff. Exports total and zone finger counts for conditional remapping; no ready-made tap-versus-physical-click recognizer found in these sources. Useful device/lifecycle reference, but requiring Karabiner/service integration does not solve Rebind's recognition requirement. Not adopted or built; repository activity is not a claim that the extension itself changed on that date.
- [BetterTouchTool trigger catalog](https://docs.folivora.ai/docs/json/trigger-definitions/): explicitly distinguishes 3/4-finger taps, clicks and double taps, plus one-finger corner taps. Its scripting/plugin facilities operate within BTT; the reviewed documentation does not supply an embeddable recognizer. Suitable optional behavior comparison, not a required external app for Rebind. Commercial application; no code/SDK license adopted, installation or runtime check performed.
- [WheelClick](https://wheelclick.app/): successor author claims trackpad three-finger tap/click uses public APIs and distinguishes native Three Finger Drag via pressure readings. This is a lead to investigate, not proof that a general public API exposes our requested global 2/3/4 contacts/corners or that pressure alone is sufficient. [Community repository](https://github.com/artginzburg/WheelClick-Community/tree/0d9dee48e0b15651b02d37d0149b512747617e07) inspected via tree/metadata: gesture engine source or an embeddable SDK/license was not found; contains releases/community/localization/upgrader/performance material. Author states macOS 13+; commercial app with free three-finger features. No app installed, measurements or source-level claim about its recognizer.

**Decision:** ready-made apps and useful source implementations do exist; do not say none exist. Prioritize adapting suitable Sanne MIT recognition logic after reviewing its missing safety/click cases, and investigate the public-API lead before committing to a production private adapter. Keep the pinned standalone Subsurface probe for measuring our hardware, not as evidence that its short-contact candidates already distinguish taps and clicks. An off-the-shelf embeddable component satisfying the whole compact scope and safety contract has not been established by this review. Production integration, click exclusivity, suppression and T1 remain unchecked. Only PLAN changed in this session; `git diff --check` is the relevant verification, no builds/tests rerun or previous test counts claimed as new results.

### Phase 4 current-app window cycling — 2026-10-09

- Scope: SPEC §5 Windows/applications, A1/A2, §§2, 6–7, 9–12. Apple [AXMain](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/applicationservices/kaxmainattribute), [AXRaise](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/applicationservices/kaxraiseaction), [client APIs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/applicationservices/axuielement_h) and actual SDK 27 AX headers establish public window enumeration/count, role/subrole/minimized/modal reads, writable main, action support/raise and focus readback. Main does not imply keyboard focus. Some DocC requests returned JS shells/one enumeration page failed; installed header declarations/builds supply API evidence, not live app behavior.
- Rechecked pinned WindowKit native raise/controller/manifest/full MIT license and [SPI listing](https://swiftpackageindex.com/naviapps/window-kit); its MainActor discovery/control architecture is unnecessary here. Newly retrieved Swindler full manifest/license clarifies its older AXSwift/PromiseKit model; its SPI fetch failed. Hammerspoon's pinned native Main/raise methods/license provide comparison. Dependency register records maintenance/platforms/decisions; no library/source copied or private window-ID mapping adopted.
- A fresh bounded public AX list supplies ordinary eligible windows; stable worker-owned order prevents repeatedly toggling only the two topmost windows when raising reorders AXWindows. Maximum 32 app-reported windows, no window titles/document data, persistence/global observers or cross-actor AX elements. Reset on eligible membership/app/revision changes; commit only after focus readback. Single window is a no-op. AX reads/writes stay on the existing serial worker with its 450 ms aggregate/150 ms per-call budget; final cancellation/ownership/focus checks before each write, no retries or unsafe rollback after a partial change.
- Only current-app window cycling is implemented in this slice. AX lists may span Spaces/fullscreen; macOS owns transitions and exact system cycle order is not promised. Modal/unknown/nonstandard/minimized/foreign elements are excluded; attached sheets/application-specific focus behavior remain real-device checks. No automatic unminimize, tab/app switcher, private window identification, Screen Recording permission, shell/keyboard fallback or new package. [Contract/walkthrough](verification/phase-4-window-cycle.md).

### Phase 4 screen-lock decision — 2026-10-09

- Scope: SPEC §5 Spaces/system, A1/A2 and §§2, 6–7, 9–12. Apple documents [Control-Command-Q](https://support.apple.com/en-us/102650), the public [GUI-session copy API](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgsessioncopycurrentdictionary()) and [inactive-session notification](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/sessiondidresignactivenotification). No documented direct local screen-lock API identified; MDM Device Lock is not a local remapper API. The shortcut risks customization/delivery issues in the user's Windows-keyboard remap setup; no keyboard/AppleScript/CGSession subprocess/screensaver/sleep fallback.
- GitHub and Swift Package Index searches did not establish a smaller maintained Swift lock package. Hammerspoon, macos-lock and MacLock independently use `SACLockScreenImmediate` with a void ABI. Chromium's [login-util comments](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/base/mac/login_util.cc) mention OSStatus without invoking this function; do not interpret a return register or advertise request acknowledgement. Full pinned MIT license/source/maintenance review in dependency register; no library/executable/source copied. Our existing optional dynamic-adapter pattern fits one entry point.
- Private login.framework entry point, lock metadata key and lock/unlock notification names are isolated in two small files. `CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary`/console key are public (SDK 27 `CGSession.h`, macOS 10.3+). Read only booleans, discard dictionary immediately; never log/store user identity or typed content. Null/non-console/malformed session fails closed; absent lock key means unlocked in the known console session, supported by MacLock and the local unlocked probe only. Future metadata changes remain a manual compatibility gate.
- [Apple distributed-notification documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/foundation/distributednotificationcenter?language=objc) confirms modern main-thread delivery, unbounded latency/drop risk and insecure interprocess messages. Treat lock notification as a suspension hint; native confirmation is required to resume. Existing MainActor poll (nominal 0.5 s, busy-run-loop delay possible) supplies a fallback; startup checks occur before workers. No session query/dynamic loading in the tap and no claim that notifications form a security boundary.
- Restrict private lock dispatch to reviewed major versions 26/27 plus symbol presence; both are experimental, not hardware-verified. Function runs outside UI/callback/mutex; no cancellation/timeout after invocation. Returned call drops queued/temporal work and diagnostic data, not the saved enabled/profile preference. Existing independent sleep/inactive guards remain. No root/helper, TCC/password/power preference changes, settings writes or auto-unlock. [Contract and actual-device gates](verification/phase-4-lock-screen.md).

> Updated from Apple documentation/installed SDK and reviewed upstream sources on 2026-10-08. These establish candidate mechanisms, **not verified Rebind hardware behavior**. No input capture or synthesis was run during preparation.

### Native API comparison and provisional first-slice direction

- **Keyboard/mouse filtering:** prefer a small native Core Graphics adapter for Phase 1. Active taps can pass through, replace or drop events; passive `listenOnly` taps cannot filter. Handle key down/up, flags changes, other-mouse down/up/drag and scroll, tap disable/timeout, and tagged synthetic input. The callback belongs to its CFRunLoop and must remain synchronous. [Quartz Event Services](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/quartz-event-services), [callback contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventtapcallback), and installed `CGEvent.h` / `CGEventTypes.h` inspected. No framework wrapper needed merely to create a tap; Phase 1 now implements lifecycle, matching and dispatch, while actual capture/suppression still needs permission/hardware checks.
- **API discrepancy:** Apple [tapCreate documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgevent/tapcreate(tap:place:options:eventsofinterest:callback:userinfo:)) and SDK header still describe root-only HID-level placement, while current LinearMouse and pqrs examples use HID taps in permission-gated apps. Do not resolve this by adding root privileges. Start with a session-level tap; measure interception order/permission behavior on the target OS before choosing a lower placement or making suppression claims.
- **AppKit:** `NSEvent` is useful for app-local recording and UI/platform integration. A global monitor is observation-only, excludes the app's own events, and cannot replace the interception engine. [Apple event-monitor guide](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/Cocoa/Conceptual/EventOverview/MonitoringEvents/MonitoringEvents.html); SDK `NSEvent.h` agrees.
- **HID:** public `IOHIDManager` provides device matching/removal callbacks, element/report values, vendor/product identity and button usages. Enumeration/non-seizing capture does not suppress Quartz delivery. Seizing is a different architecture with failure/re-emission risks; defer. [IOHIDManager](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/iokit/iohidmanager_h), SDK `hid/IOHIDManager.h`, and pqrs examples inspected. Keep per-device discovery separate from reliable per-event attribution.
- **Permissions:** check `AXIsProcessTrusted` / `AXIsProcessTrustedWithOptions`, `CGPreflightListenEventAccess`, `CGPreflightPostEventAccess`, and method-appropriate `IOHIDCheckAccess`. Request only the selected capture/action's access; verify state and actual tap creation after System Settings. A prompt is asynchronous and does not make AX trust true. [AX trust](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/applicationservices/1459186-axisprocesstrustedwithoptions), [listen preflight](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgpreflightlisteneventaccess()), [post preflight](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgpreflightposteventaccess()), [IOHID access](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/iokit/3181573-iohidcheckaccess), [Apple consent overview](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2019/701/). The initial Phase 1 gate required AX/listen/post. See the permission correction below: AX/post govern the current active tap, listening remains informational. Future HID implementations must check their own access needs. No permission was requested or granted during preparation.
- **Secure Input:** Apple explains that secure keyboard contexts withhold events and exposes `IsSecureEventInputEnabled`. Fail safely; never log text, read passwords, or disable Secure Input to regain capture. [TN2150](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/technotes/tn2150/_index.html) is archived guidance, not proof of current Rebind behavior; modern protected contexts require manual checks.
- **Native actions/lifecycle:** prefer `AXUIElement` for supported window operations, `NSWorkspace` for opening/activating apps and URLs, Core Audio for supported output-volume properties, and `SMAppService.mainApp` for launch-at-login. Verify action-level support and errors; output shortcuts can depend on user keyboard settings. [AX client APIs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/applicationservices/axuielement_h), [NSWorkspace](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace), [Core Audio](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio), [SMAppService](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/smappservice). Apple's SMAppService DocC JSON was also retrieved to bypass the JavaScript-only web shell. No public universal Space-switch or media-control mechanism was established; defer those specific actions until validated. SwiftUI/AppKit own menu-bar/window lifecycle; no helper is currently justified.
- **Performance:** maintain a small event mask and synchronous allocation-light matcher; bound any diagnostic/action handoff and stream buffering. Package convenience does not establish callback safety. For private touch adapters, audit C-buffer lifetime, callback cancellation, hotplug/wake behavior and concurrency before selection; do not copy singleton/force-unwrap/main-queue patterns blindly.

### Phase 3 native HID discovery decision — 2026-10-08 (retired)

> Historical research only. The user withdrew device inventory and source attribution on 2026-10-08; their implementation, UI and tests have been removed. Manual profiles replace this Phase 3 scope. The findings below describe the earlier decision, not current functionality or an outstanding implementation gate.

- Scope SPEC §§3.2–3.3, D1/D2, 6–7, 10–12: inspect live HID interfaces and their declared capabilities without a mouse-model catalog or per-device binding claim. Apple [CoreHID discovery](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/corehid/hiddevicemanager) and [communication guide](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/corehid/communicatingwithhiddevices) provide native actors and an asynchronous match/removal stream. Installed SDK 27 interface confirms macOS 15 availability, checked-Sendable device references/usages and actor-isolated metadata; macOS 26 minimum is unchanged. Native CoreHID replaces the need for custom C callbacks/run-loop ownership or periodic enumeration for this slice.
- Compare public [IOHIDManager](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/iokit/iohidmanager_h) and [CopyDevices](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/iokit/1438391-iohidmanagercopydevices), [hidapi](https://github.com/libusb/hidapi) macOS backend, USBDeviceSwift and existing LinearMouse/pqrs/Karabiner references. Choose CoreHID plus a small public registry metadata reader; no wrapper, helper, private API, runtime package or copied implementation.
- Actual metadata-only probes find six relevant interfaces, with repeated matches for composite criteria. `HIDDeviceClient.elements` returns empty; `IOHIDDeviceCopyMatchingElements` returns nil without opening devices. Public `kIOHIDElementKey` registry properties contain collection headers but no individual input elements on this host. The cause of unavailable individual elements is not established. Do not open/seize devices, request input/report values, parse the binary report descriptor ourselves, add an input subscription or prompt for another permission just to fill the inventory. Available individual element metadata is supported; absent/incomplete metadata is explicitly unknown. Descriptor declarations are not evidence that G HUB delivers corresponding remappable events.
- Metadata acquisition/registry work runs outside MainActor using `@concurrent`; native actors own framework state. One owned cancellable discovery task, weak UI ownership, generation checks against stale updates, deduplicated live IDs and cleared stale rows on stream failure. No work is added to the synchronous Quartz callback; no typed input, event values, serial numbers, hardware identities persisted, file writes, telemetry or new permission requests. No explicit device-open/seize APIs are called; framework-internal resource setup is owned by CoreHID.
- Receiver name/connection reflect the HID interface, not the online state/model of its wireless mouse. Multiple interfaces and identical product IDs are kept separate; IDs are current-connection identifiers only. Trackpad classification uses explicit HID usages/collection metadata, not a product-name heuristic. Magic Mouse can appear as a mouse interface if exposed; this does not enable its touch gestures. Category-wide rules remain unchanged; physical-device attribution/reconciliation is a separate gate.
- Research limits: SPI HID searches were attempted, but its direct search page failed to fetch and indexed searches did not expose an applicable candidate; this does not prove no package exists. USBDeviceSwift latest-commit page/raw manifest requests failed (GitHub manifest body was inspected). One follow-up internet search failed with a connection error; no findings are claimed from it. Current SDK declarations, successful primary-source pages and actual native probes/builds supply the decision evidence.

### Phase 4 shared action catalog / audio decision — 2026-10-08

- Scope SPEC §§2, 4–6, 9–12, A1/A2. Native [Core Audio](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio), [default output](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio/kaudiohardwarepropertydefaultoutputdevice), [VolumeScalar](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio/kaudiodevicepropertyvolumescalar), [Mute](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio/kaudiodevicepropertymute), [settable check](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio/audioobjectispropertysettable(_:_:_:)) and [setter](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreaudio/audioobjectsetpropertydata(_:_:_:_:_:_:)) plus installed SDK 27 `AudioHardware.h` inspected. Apple pages returned JavaScript shells; SDK declarations establish main element, Float32 0…1 scalar, UInt32 mute, actual settable checks and OSStatus results. No microphone capture, input subscription, private API, helper, external script or additional TCC permission.
- Investigated [SimplyCoreAudio](https://github.com/rnine/SimplyCoreAudio): archived 2024-03-23, repository identifies MIT; [manifest](https://github.com/rnine/SimplyCoreAudio/blob/develop/Package.swift) tools 5.1/macOS 10.12, Swift 4/4.2/5 modes and Swift Atomics + C target. No Swift 6.4/local package build audit. Its direct SPI page and full license fetches failed; no license-text review claimed. Not adopted: archived general-purpose device/stream/notification framework for three narrowly scoped output properties.
- Compared [CAAudioHardware](https://github.com/sbooth/CAAudioHardware), [manifest](https://github.com/sbooth/CAAudioHardware/blob/main/Package.swift), [AudioDevice](https://github.com/sbooth/CAAudioHardware/blob/main/Sources/CAAudioHardware/AudioDevice.swift) and [SPI](https://swiftpackageindex.com/sbooth/CAAudioHardware). Public non-archived HAL wrapper; MIT identified by upstream/source headers, tools 6.0/macOS 10.15, CoreAudioExtensions dependency. Retrieved SPI snapshot last updated 2026-02-05 lists release 0.7.1 and recent branch/issue activity relative to that snapshot; exact current commit date and local Swift 6.4 compatibility unverified. Full license URLs failed. Not adopted: full object/property/notification hierarchy and transitive package are unnecessary for the bounded main-element adapter. No third-party code copied. GitHub/SPI searches were broader than the SPEC candidate list; failed guessed URLs are not evidence no package exists.
- SDK `AudioHardwareService.h` exposes virtual main volume but its service functions are deprecated as unsupported since 10.11; this slice uses only current `AudioObject…` APIs and writable main-element controls. Channel-only, fixed-volume, HDMI/digital or absent controls are unavailable when these actual queries fail; do not infer capability from transport/model or flatten independent channels. Default output identity remains transient/internal, never a device list or persistent profile source.
- Runtime owns one additional in-process serial action thread for synchronous HAL/Quartz dispatch, with one bounded 16-entry mailbox and at most one accepted call in flight. No Core Audio I/O, posting, Task or actor hop in the tap callback; no unbounded async jobs. Capabilities refresh at most once per second and after action failure, with read/set/default-device rechecks before a write. Capability/output/config/edit/pause/capture-loss transitions cancel waiting work and recognition while retaining consumed releases where capture remains available. Already accepted platform calls cannot be recalled; real latency/reconnect/permission races remain hardware checks.
- This is the first Phase 4 slice, not completion of windows/Spaces/Mission Control/playback/open app/URL/mouse synthesis. Built-in volume change does not unmute; toggle mute is explicit. Errors have localized UI feedback; HAL acceptance is not acoustic acknowledgement. Schema 7 preserves schema-6 profiles/selected UUIDs and schemas 1–5 legacy rules in memory; no silent file rewrite or action masquerading in old schema.

### Phase 4 selected app / web / file opening decision — 2026-10-08

- Scope SPEC §5 A1/A2, §§9–12. Researched public [NSWorkspace application opening](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/openapplication(at:configuration:completionhandler:)), [URL opening](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/open(_:configuration:completionhandler:)), [OpenConfiguration](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/openconfiguration) and [NSRunningApplication activation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsrunningapplication/activate(options:)). JavaScript-only pages and failed Markdown fetches did not provide full declarations; installed SDK 27 `NSWorkspace.h` / `NSRunningApplication.h` establish availability (modern opening since macOS 10.15), configuration semantics and deprecated activation options. Successful Swift 6.4 builds confirm the async imports. Deployment 26 unchanged; no macOS 26 execution claim.
- Native APIs satisfy this slice without helpers, scripts, a process launcher, private APIs or added permissions. Use one MainActor async native request, bridged by existing UI polling through a single claimed mailbox slot. File/bundle metadata preparation stays on the serial action thread; no I/O, Task or actor hop enters the synchronous input callback. Serial output waits until native completion; 16 waiting entries plus one accepted request remain bounded. No speculative native timeout/retry that could duplicate an already accepted request.
- Opening activates the selected app with no new-instance request or substitution by another running copy. Activation checks running bundle ID and standardized path and never launches. Directory URL hints/trailing slashes are normalized by path comparison. File targets use default handlers, folders use the system handler, and web targets allow explicit HTTP/HTTPS only. Custom schemes/Apple Shortcuts remain separate unfinished scope. Paths and app IDs are stored locally as explicit action parameters; contents, request paths and URL queries are not logged in diagnostics. Moves need reselection; no bookmark recovery or executable/code-signing identity claim.
- Set `promptsUserIfNeeded = false`, disable recent-item insertion and preserve OS security. SDK documentation explicitly says this flag does **not** suppress Gatekeeper UI. Accepted Launch Services requests cannot be recalled after pause/profile/sleep changes; stale completion errors are ignored. A native request waiting on Gatekeeper/completion can delay subsequent actions until it finishes; the tap and pause UI remain independent. Filesystem metadata (including a mounted network path) also has no hard latency guarantee. Actual responsiveness/ordering/latency need manual measurement.
- Investigated [SwifterSwift](https://github.com/SwifterSwift/SwifterSwift), [SPI listing](https://swiftpackageindex.com/SwifterSwift/SwifterSwift), raw manifest and full MIT license: broad extension framework, tools 6.0/macOS 10.13, separate AppKit product, no external manifest dependencies observed. SPI snapshot lists 8.0.0; cached commit history shows activity 2026-07-21, not a verified current head. Guessed NSWorkspace extension/source-directory fetches failed, so no launcher-wrapper source audit is claimed. **Not adopted:** no demonstrated benefit over native NSWorkspace plus the required cancellation/serialization contract; no package build/Swift 6 concurrency audit or copied code.
- Compared [lgrammel/app-launcher](https://github.com/lgrammel/app-launcher): README, raw manifest, full MIT license and native Swift source inspected. A complete executable app with one visible repository commit, tools 6.3/macOS 26.5 and no external manifest packages. Its MainActor async NSWorkspace application opening is a useful API reference, but its product/toolchain floor and whole-app scope do not justify a dependency. Latest commit date/local compatibility were not established. **Not adopted; no code copied**, and its unrelated Process/AppleScript features are not used.
