Complete all releases once started, no sleep/Task/held output or forced cursor move. No delivery acknowledgement; rapid pointer/physical-button races and real target double-click handling are manual gates.
- Shared translated Mouse category/four actions/hint/error, no parameters or output-shortcut recorder. Preparation failure does not emergency-pause other rules or replay consumed input. Schema 14 accepts 1–13 with all-profile/selection/action/activation preservation and no read-time rewrite; reject disguised mouse actions in every older schema/inactive profile and unknown payloads. Dragging/held output, modified clicks, back/forward, wheel synthesis remain unimplemented. [Contract and hardware gates](verification/phase-4-mouse-output.md).

### Phase 4 Apple Shortcuts decision — 2026-10-09

- Research: Apple's [CLI guide](https://support.apple.com/en-gb/guide/shortcuts-mac/apd455c82f02/mac), [URL scheme guide](https://support.apple.com/en-gb/guide/shortcuts-mac/apd624386f42/mac), [Foundation Process](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/foundation/process), GitHub and Swift Package Index. Installed `shortcuts --help`, `list --help` and `run --help` establish `list --show-identifiers` and `run <shortcut-name-or-identifier>` on this Mac. A read-only native list returned status 0, two rows, both UUID-suffixed; names/contents were not printed and no workflow ran. Apple warns interactive workflows may wait on prompts/input. The documented URL scheme names a shortcut and offers text/clipboard input; it does not establish the same stable-ID/exit-status contract. Choose CLI UUID arguments, never name fallback or custom web schemes.
- Evaluated official [swiftlang/swift-subprocess](https://github.com/swiftlang/swift-subprocess), [SPI listing](https://swiftpackageindex.com/swiftlang/swift-subprocess) and [1.0.0 release](https://github.com/swiftlang/swift-subprocess/releases/tag/1.0.0). Current main manifest requires tools 6.2, macOS 13, Swift 6; uses C shims/kqueue and conditional non-Apple swift-system dependency. Full [Apache-2.0 license](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/swiftlang/swift-subprocess/main/LICENSE) read. Official repository has a stable 1.0.0 release and subsequent commits; exact latest commit date/local compatibility build not established. Suitable maintained candidate, deferred for this narrow synchronous-worker integration: two fixed commands and one capped pipe fit Foundation Process without adding an async subprocess framework/C target. No package installed or source copied; reconsider for broader subprocess needs.
- Explicit Picker/Refresh reads only names/IDs on a cancellable off-MainActor task. Bound UTF-8 listing to 256 KiB/4096 rows, names to 512 UTF-8 bytes/no controls; reject malformed or duplicate IDs. Persist UUID and display name, refresh renames by ID, preserve/explain missing saved selection. Schema 10 reads 1–9 without rewriting; old-schema disguised actions fail validation even in inactive profiles.
- Runtime uses `/usr/bin/shortcuts` plus separate `run`, UUID arguments on the existing serial worker. No shell, clipboard/file input or retained workflow output; stdin/stdout/stderr go to `/dev/null`. Poll current generation, posting permission and Secure Input while waiting; timeout 60 seconds, listing 10 seconds. Terminate only the owned CLI with SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 250 ms if still running, and reap. Process launch/termination can add latency; this is a client waiting limit, not a hard OS/service cancellation guarantee or rollback. UI explains that accepted steps may finish; other output waits with the existing 16-item bound. No automatic retry or grant of workflow permissions.
- Implemented and software-tested; native listing and Russian picker inspected on this host. Physical-trigger execution, other locale/accessibility checks, rename/delete, prompts, cancellation and timing remain manual gates. [Contract and walkthrough](verification/phase-4-shortcuts.md).

### Phase 1 native implementation decision — 2026-10-08

- Rechecked [Apple CGEvent/tap creation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgevent), [callback contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventtapcallback), [permission preflight](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgpreflightlisteneventaccess()), [Secure Input TN2150](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/technotes/tn2150/_index.html), [pqrs observer examples](https://github.com/pqrs-org/osx-event-observer-examples), [LinearMouse button-numbering documentation](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/Documentation/Configuration.md), [KeyboardShortcuts upstream](https://github.com/sindresorhus/KeyboardShortcuts) and its [Swift Package Index listing](https://swiftpackageindex.com/sindresorhus/KeyboardShortcuts). Existing maintenance/license/source audit in the register still applies; no new version-specific package compatibility claim. One pqrs source-page request failed; do not claim a new source review of that file.
- Installed SDK headers confirm the C callback's synchronous lifetime, nullable tap, disable event types, source-user-data field, public listen/post preflight/request APIs and `IsSecureEventInputEnabled`. Some Apple DocC pages returned a JavaScript shell; SDK declarations and previously reviewed documentation supplied the API evidence.
- Choose **public active session event tap**, no root/HID seizure/private API/helper. AppKit global monitors cannot suppress; Carbon hotkey wrappers are insufficient for arbitrary physical key + extra-button interception. Existing MIT KeyboardShortcuts/macOS 10.15+/Swift 6.2 candidate remains deferred; adding registered-hotkey storage is unnecessary for the shared metadata recorder. No dependency installed or third-party runtime copied.
- Dedicated worker owns tap/CF source/context; checked-Sendable Mutex mailbox crosses threads. No `Task`, UI access, file I/O, text extraction or posting in the callback. Bounded 16-entry pulse queue drains outside callbacks. Source repeats/drags/releases are consumed only for owned suppressed presses. Unmatched/ambiguous/overflow input passes through.
- Output uses exact flags on a tagged private-source key-down/up pulse, with no synthetic modifier key-downs/held state. Its effectiveness and modifier coexistence need manual recipient testing; no Quartz delivery acknowledgement exists. Do not extend to held output without a separate state machine.
- Initially AX trust plus listen/post preflight gated tap startup. The redundant listen requirement is corrected below: this active Quartz engine needs AX trust and actual post access. Secure Input is checked before matching and output. Tap-disable and output failures cancel queued actions and require explicit retry; sleep/session suspension clears acquisition state. Permission revocation, sleep/wake and protected contexts remain hardware/OS-unverified.
- Small Phase 7 prerequisites are included so configured rules survive relaunch and the engine outlives its window: atomic version-1 JSON, corrupt/future-file editing lock and menu-bar lifecycle. This does **not** complete Phase 7 (recovery/import/export/migrations/login/settings remain open).

### Permission correction and generic G603 strategy — 2026-10-08

- Scope: Phase 1 hardware/onboarding gate; `SPEC.md` §7, S2, M1/M2 and Phase 3 capability planning. The user reports granting Accessibility but seeing no response from Input Monitoring requests. This does not prove the running bundle has access.
- [Apple DTS explains](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/828052) that Accessibility already grants event listening and posting; separate Input Monitoring is unnecessary for this path. This corrects the original overly restrictive gate, rather than assuming a click grants access. Apple notes `IOHIDRequestAccess` for HID-specific requests. The [pqrs observer README](https://github.com/pqrs-org/osx-event-observer-examples) says both permissions are needed, illustrating why generic older examples cannot define the current gate. Native SDK public AX/Quartz APIs remain the implementation; no dependency added.
- Shared nonisolated `InputPermissions` snapshot keeps UI and worker policy consistent: require actual AX trust and post access; retain actual listen preflight as informational. Still require successful real tap creation; no fallback to an unsuppressible observer, bypass of TCC, or fake authorization. Secure Input, permission-loss and tap-failure handling remain intact.
- Remove the redundant Input Monitoring request button. Explain its absence in EN/RU/DE/FR; show explicit granted/missing status, a posting-denial hint, correct-copy/relaunch guidance and Show App in Finder. An explicit Accessibility request opens its System Settings pane when access remains missing, including when macOS does not repeat the prompt.
- G603 needs no model-specific whitelist for standard mouse events: current matcher accepts numeric Quartz middle/extra button IDs without a fixed button enum. [Logitech specifications](https://support.logi.com/hc/en-us/articles/360023463533-G603-LIGHTSPEED-Wireless-Gaming-Mouse-Technical-Specifications) list left/right, wheel, forward/back, DPI and a separate underside connectivity button. **Physical/control count is not the count of OS-visible buttons**; DPI/connectivity may be handled locally (hypothesis until measured). Phase 3 will report advertised HID button usages separately from actually observed Quartz buttons; neither enumeration nor attribution is implemented yet. User confirmed LIGHTSPEED and installed G HUB, then Control + Button 4 → Command+A in TextEdit and plain Button 4 behavior; other hardware mappings remain unverified.
- Rechecked native APIs, LinearMouse MIT generic [button configuration](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/Documentation/Configuration.md), pqrs Unlicense examples and the existing maintenance/license register. Swift Package Index HID/mouse search returned no applicable new candidate; do not infer none exists. Existing generic Quartz capture is the smallest current solution. No plugins, drivers, G HUB, vendor protocols or packages installed.

### Separate synthesis-consent step — 2026-10-08

- User screenshots now show **AX granted, listen preflight unavailable, post preflight unavailable** and disabled recording. Logitech G603 is connected via LIGHTSPEED, with G HUB installed. This is stronger evidence than the previous missing-AX observation, but still no recorded physical event or successful output delivery.
- Retain the real post preflight gate. [Apple DTS](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/744440) recommends the specific Quartz preflight/request APIs for event taps; installed SDK `CGEvent.h` declares `CGRequestPostEventAccess` as the synthesis access request. The previous AX button requested synthesis only when AX was already trusted at click time; granting AX later did not advance to an explicit synthesis request. The UI instead implied AX was missing or told the user only to relaunch. This workflow is corrected.
- New typed missing-access state advances AX → event posting → ready based solely on real checks. Dedicated **Allow Event Sending** button invokes `CGRequestPostEventAccess` on user action, refreshes and opens Accessibility settings if access stays unavailable. Already-granted AX request is disabled. Main window, menu bar and editor report the actual missing stage. No automated permission grant, startup prompt, TCC reset or bypass; no global-event probe was posted.
- [A current Apple forum report](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/842985) describes AX/PostEvent disagreement with managed macOS 27 permissions; an Apple engineer calls its multiple-alert behavior a likely bug. This does **not** establish that this Mac is managed or has the same bug. Request/relaunch results on the user's exact copy are still needed; do not silently assume AX makes post preflight true.
- Permission explanations wrap vertically; a bounded native scroll view prevents the permission panel from consuming the entire small window. Screenshot truncation is addressed in code, with native resized-window/VoiceOver verification pending. No dependencies or helpers added; existing native API and skill decisions stand.

### Phase 2 discrete wheel decision — 2026-10-08

- Investigated Apple [line-delta fields](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventfield/scrollwheeleventdeltaaxis1), [timestamps](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgevent/timestamp) and installed SDK `CGEventTypes.h`; line axis 1 is vertical, axis 2 horizontal, timestamps approximately nanoseconds since startup. Continuous, scroll-phase, momentum-phase and third-axis fields provide a conservative eligibility filter. Any nonzero phase is left untouched; no undocumented phase-value interpretation is needed. [Hammerspoon's scroll-event documentation](https://www.hammerspoon.org/docs/hs.eventtap.event.html#newScrollEvent) supports positive up/left and negative down/right. These describe delivered events, not guaranteed physical-device identity.
- Extended the existing public session tap and bounded mailbox instead of adding another event source. `ScrollEventAdapter` reads safe numeric metadata only; matcher emits at most one shortcut per 100 ms across wheel rules, swallowing intermediate matched pulses without delayed accumulation. Unsupported, unmatched, paused, overloaded and regressed-time inputs pass through. No posting, actor hop, timer creation, I/O or UI in the callback; output still drains on the existing worker. No private API or new permission.
- Compared [UnnaturalScrollWheels source](https://github.com/ther0n/UnnaturalScrollWheels/blob/main/UnnaturalScrollWheels/ScrollInterceptor.swift), its GPL-3.0 license, [Mos](https://github.com/Caldis/Mos) README/license and existing MIT LinearMouse configuration/source references. UnnaturalScrollWheels discusses why the continuous flag alone cannot prove mouse/trackpad identity and includes tap/wake handling; Mos documents smoothing/conversion and per-axis behavior. Full applications and license/architecture costs do not justify importing them for this small slice. No third-party implementation was copied. A requested LinearMouse `Event/ScrollEvent.swift` URL failed; no source audit of that file is claimed.
- GitHub and Swift Package Index searches for macOS wheel/event interception found no suitable drop-in package for this extension. This is search evidence, not proof no package exists or an index compatibility result. Retain native APIs; no runtime dependency installed.
- Keep honest scope in all four languages: only discrete single-axis events, shared ten-pulse/s ceiling, OS-defined direction, transformed events can still match. Do not classify physical devices from the flag. Smooth/momentum handling, native actions and per-device routing remain open. Button-hold pointer directions are implemented in the separate slice below, with hardware checks pending.
- Added configuration schema 2 and validated in-memory migration of schema-1 key/button rules. Legacy disk contents stay intact until successful save; disguised version-1 wheel content, invalid/conflicting or future files cannot overwrite valid data. Only this necessary migration is implemented; Phase 7 recovery/import/export/general migration work remains open.

### Phase 2 button-hold pointer-direction decision — 2026-10-08

- **Product decision:** user explicitly chose left/right/up/down as the complete mouse pointer gesture set. No diagonals, spirals/circles or multi-stroke figures planned. This affects mouse button-hold pointer gestures only; separate trackpad/Magic Mouse requirements remain. Focus on reliable recognition, sensitivity and useful actions instead of adding shapes. SPEC §3.2 updated; current recognizer/editor already expose exactly this set.
- Scope: SPEC §§2, 3.2, 4, 6, 9. Use the existing public session CGEventTap and [relative X/Y fields](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventfield/mouseeventdeltax). Absolute cursor position is unsuitable for suppressed motion/display edges. Rechecked Apple [auxiliary drag](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventtype/othermousedragged) and [NSPanGestureRecognizer](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nspangesturerecognizer); installed SDK NSGestureRecognizer.h explicitly limits recognizers to hit-tested NSView/subview events, so they do not provide global suppression. DocC pages for pan/Y field returned JavaScript shells; native SDK headers supply the view/callback contract.
- Investigated current [LinearMouse ButtonMappingEngine](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LinearMouse/EventTransformer/ButtonMappingEngine.swift), [configuration](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/Documentation/Configuration.md), [MIT license](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LICENSE), [project](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LinearMouse.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj) and release activity (0.12.0 and 0.13.0-beta.1 on 2026-10-03). Its recognizer depends on application Scheme/Mapping/action types, buffered replay, chords and stateful outcomes; it is not an independent drop-in package for this slice. Swift 5 mode confirmed; prior register records macOS 10.15 generally/12 with SDK 27, no Rebind Swift 6 compatibility claim. Its 50-unit/default-policy and relative-field approach are reference evidence, not a physical calibration. No source copied or full app imported. Also inspected [GestureKit](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/tree/main/Modules/GestureKit): README describes event simulation, not pointer recognition; package fetch failed. Not adopted.
- Compared [MacGesture](https://github.com/MacGesture/MacGesture) / [GPL-3.0 license](https://github.com/MacGesture/MacGesture/blob/develop/LICENSE): Objective-C full application with direction sequences, not a reviewed Swift package; exact OS minimum/current maintenance dates were not exposed in fetched pages. No source reuse/licensing change. Also inspected [EventTapCore](https://github.com/philptr/EventTapCore): GPL-3.0-labeled async/SwiftUI event wrapper, three commits visible, latest date and OS/Swift minima unverified because manifest body/full license fetch failed. Does not supply the required recognizer or establish synchronous suppression. Existing EventTapper assessment remains applicable. No dependency added blindly.
- GitHub and Swift Package Index searches were performed for global mouse gestures and event wrappers. SPI's direct gesture search failed; domain search returned no applicable recognizer. This is limited search evidence, not proof no package exists. Native capture is already implemented and the remaining bounded directional state machine is small; choose the existing native engine with no new runtime dependency, helper, private API or permission.
- Add separate `mouseGesture(button:direction:)` triggers: four directions may share a source/modifier chord, but conflict with press/double/hold on it. Capture source down and accumulate relative integer deltas; 50 units on the dominant axis and a 2:1 dominance ratio. First substantial diagonal or unconfigured direction cancels. One pulse per owned press; incomplete clicks have no fallback/replay. Threshold is a fixed initial policy, not a point/mm guarantee or adjustable UI setting.
- Gesture capture ends at release, cancellation or five seconds from down. Worker polls expiry even without new movement; extreme/regressed input fails safely. Release ownership remains until up or capture loss. Recording captures the source button only; direction is chosen separately. Tagged feedback is ignored; bounded queue, exact modifiers, Secure Input, pause/edit/configuration and failure handling remain. Full missed-up/device-loss behavior still needs hardware checks.
- Handle driver-delivered mouseMoved as well as otherMouseDragged; add idle-motion subscription only for enabled compiled gesture rules. Recreate the tap on its worker when that mask changes, preserving owned releases; failed recreation clears acquisition/output. Native pointer behavior, event order and performance under high polling rates are unverified. Callback still has no posting, tasks/actor hops, UI, I/O or logs.
- Schema 5 migrates schemas 1–4 in memory; current user's schema-4 hold duration/IDs/output/state remain intact. Invalid/future/conflicting files cannot overwrite valid data. No project identity/deployment/security setting changes. EN/RU/DE/FR labels explain click consumption, five-second window and conflicts; Russian native editor inspected.

### Phase 2 long-press decision — 2026-10-08

- Scope: `SPEC.md` §§2, 3.1–3.2, 4, 6, 9; Phase 2 hold recognition. First slice is **one shortcut pulse after a long source press**, not synthetic keys held down or repeating actions. Per-rule threshold is 100–5000 ms, default 500 ms. Normal/double/long modes for the same enabled source/modifier chord conflict; wheel cannot use a press duration.
- Rechecked [LinearMouse configuration](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/Documentation/Configuration.md): its MIT implementation distinguishes long press from held output and repeat, and documents a 500 ms default. Existing maintenance/license audit applies; no source code copied. Swift Package Index keyboard-event searches returned UI-local/iOS or unrelated packages, not an applicable global-hold solution; this is not proof none exists. Extend the existing native matcher/worker rather than add a wrapper, helper or vendor plugin.
- One constant-size hold candidate, plus existing release ownership. A matched down is consumed immediately. Releasing before the threshold produces no output and no source replay; this tradeoff is explicit in all four UI languages. Keyboard autorepeat and dragging with the held mouse source preserve the original deadline without emitting extra pulses. Duplicate non-repeat down, changed primary/Fn modifiers, another captured press, any scroll (including unsupported smooth/momentum/diagonal), unrelated dragging, pause/edit/record/configuration change, queue saturation, Secure Input and capture loss cancel pending recognition. Releases for already consumed sources remain owned until capture loss. UI focus loss cancels explicit recording only.
- Poll the existing event worker between CF run-loop iterations; no new timer/Task/actor hop/output/UI/I/O in the callback. Queue remains capped at 16. At threshold emit once, never on a timer per source; a polling delay above 100 ms beyond the deadline cancels instead of delivering stale actions. Timing/load/device-disconnect/missing-up behavior need physical checks. Ordinary pointer motion without a drag does not cancel a long press; directional motion uses the separate button-hold gesture slice above.
- [Apple DispatchTime](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/dispatch/dispatchtime) uses a monotonic Mach clock. Receipt and polling share `uptimeNanoseconds` for holds; preserve original [Quartz timestamps](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventtimestamp) for double-press/wheel timing rather than assume clock origins match. The optional receipt timestamp keeps pure tests deterministic. [Privacy manifest reason 35F9.1](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/bundleresources/app-privacy-configuration/nsprivacyaccessedapitypes/nsprivacyaccessedapitype) declares elapsed-time/timer calculations; no tracking, collected data or off-device timestamps.
- Configuration schema 4 reads schemas 1/2/3 in memory, preserving stable IDs/enabled state/output/activation. Old durations default to 500 ms; legacy hold modes, wheel holds, out-of-range/malformed durations and enabled-mode conflicts reject without overwriting data. Native editor copies existing durations, shows a stepper only for holds, resets on source-type changes, and keeps cancellation hints readable. No project identity/deployment/security changes.

### Phase 2 double-press decision — 2026-10-08

- Rechecked [Apple `NSEvent.doubleClickInterval`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsevent/doubleclickinterval), [Quartz click-state field](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventfield/mouseeventclickstate) and installed `NSEvent.h`/`CGEventTypes.h`. AppKit exposes the system interval; Quartz click state is mouse-specific, not a keyboard-pair mechanism. Choose delivered timestamps + required release for consistent ordinary-key/extra-button **double press**, rather than claiming native spatial double/triple click. Interval read on MainActor during configuration, not in the synchronous callback; only invalid/unexpected values use the 500 ms fallback.
- Searched GitHub and Swift Package Index. [SwiftKeys](https://github.com/jordanbaird/SwiftKeys) explicitly offers key-down/up and configurable double-tap observation plus a recorder; its [manifest](https://github.com/jordanbaird/SwiftKeys/blob/main/Package.swift) declares Swift tools 5.9/macOS 10.13, MIT and no dependencies. [SPI listing](https://swiftpackageindex.com/jordanbaird/SwiftKeys) reports last update 2023-12-19. Useful reference, but its key-command/UserDefaults subsystem does not establish arbitrary mouse suppression, existing press ownership or Rebind's cancellation contract. A source-directory fetch returned HTTP 429; no implementation/concurrency audit claimed.
- Also compared [EventTapper](https://github.com/usagimaru/EventTapper), a MIT-labeled native keyboard/mouse tap wrapper. README confirms filtering; manifest/license-source requests failed and fetched GitHub page did not expose latest commit dates. No modern compatibility/maintenance claim or source reuse; replacing the existing worker with another tap owner would not implement the required state machine. Existing pqrs observer/KeyboardShortcuts audit remains applicable. No package installed or third-party code copied.
- Keep a single constant-size temporal candidate and the existing suppression set. First matched press is consumed immediately; an eligible second down queues one balanced shortcut. No timers, pending single replay, sleeps, text collection, tasks or actor hops in the callback. Ordinary press and double press for one enabled source/modifier chord conflict. Tagged Rebind feedback is ignored. Modifier changes and unrelated button/key presses cancel; primary mouse down is observed for cancellation only and never mapped.
- Mailbox now evaluates each event exactly once; its former disabled pre-pass would erase temporal state. Pause/configuration/recording/editing preserve swallowed-source release ownership while clearing the candidate/output queue. Capture loss/Secure Input/suspension still fail open and clear acquisition. Tests verify these policies, not actual protected-context or driver behavior.
- Schema 3 adds activation with default ordinary press for missing legacy fields. Schema 1/2 migrate in memory, keep disk untouched until successful atomic save, and reject disguised double rules. Double wheel activation, unknown/null enum values and conflicting enabled activation modes are rejected. Hold/triple-click/Caps Lock/Fn-only/physical-device recognition remain open.

### Risk R-01 - Trackpad raw contacts vs OS gesture suppression

- **Source evidence:** OpenMultitouchSupport currently uses `MTDeviceCreateDefault`, a private binary framework, Swift tools 6.2/macOS 15+, and requires sandbox off. Its Swift adapter maps frames and formats timestamps; audit overhead/ownership before use. Subsurface supports multiple-device monitoring and recognition; its source also classifies Magic Mouse, but has private API and dependency risks.
- **Unknown:** actual four-finger tap behavior, conflicts and suppressibility on target macOS/mac hardware.
- **Action:** spike on hardware before claiming replacement behavior; isolate private API adapter. Sanne explicitly describes system-gesture conflicts and advises disabling overlapping OS gestures; contact callbacks are not suppression proof. [Sanne guidance](https://github.com/anandghegde/sanne), [OpenMultitouchSupport](https://github.com/Kyome22/OpenMultitouchSupport), [Subsurface](https://github.com/mrkai77/Subsurface).
- **Status:** Researching (no Rebind implementation/test evidence yet).

### Risk R-02 - Physical device routing withdrawn

- Prior source research found public Quartz source process/state fields do not establish mouse identity; LinearMouse's private event/sender APIs were isolated in an experimental probe without fallback. The user supplied a G603 diagnostics screenshot with Button 4/5 events showing Unknown, then explicitly chose removal and manual profiles.
- Native enumeration had worked and receiver disappearance was user-confirmed. Unknown sender values do **not** prove macOS cannot distinguish any device; enumeration was not attribution. These are archived findings, not outstanding product gates.
- **Decision:** remove inventory/probe/private APIs/per-device routing, retain category-wide input, one manually selected profile. No automatic hardware profile selection. Revisit only on a new explicit user request.
- **Status:** Closed by scope decision, not a universal platform Unsupported claim.

### Risk R-03 - Extra mouse buttons and vendor drivers

- **Source evidence:** SDK `NSEvent.buttonNumber` and `CGEventField.mouseEventButtonNumber` expose numeric values; HID button usage is separate. Common Quartz/AppKit primary/secondary/middle indexes are 0/1/2, while human/HID Button 1/2/3 labels are typically one-based. Normalize verified events, preserve unknown numbers, and do not import another app's fixed maximum. [NSEvent](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsevent), [LinearMouse mapping documentation](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/03b0656dc5e4364ee951449e0851a029a25fd5be/Documentation/Configuration.md). OEM remapping/HID++ can emit keys or consume events instead of mouse-button events.
- **Unknown:** behavior across specific user hardware and macOS installations.
- **Action:** include raw button-number diagnostics and test actual side buttons; don't hard-code maxima.
- **Status:** Researching.

### Risk R-04 - Magic Mouse multitouch

- **Source evidence:** MIT Sanne enumerates private MultitouchSupport devices and separates mouse/trackpad acquisition. Apache-2.0 Subsurface's device source recognizes Magic Mouse families with a built-in-device caveat. These are alternatives to GPL-3.0-only Magic Mouse Toolkit; none establishes Rebind hardware support. The GPL toolkit also documents permission-revocation hazards, reinforcing the need for fail-open design rather than importing its runtime.
- **Unknown:** actual capture, suppression and compatibility for requested gestures.
- **Action:** research MIT/public/private alternatives and test on real Magic Mouse; honor license obligations.
- **Status:** Researching.

### Risk R-05 - System actions and Space switching

- **Source evidence:** native AX/NSWorkspace/ServiceManagement cover discrete responsibilities. HotKey and KeyboardShortcuts register Carbon hotkeys; they are not arbitrary key/button remapping engines. LinearMouse's DockKit includes private declarations, so do not treat it as a public universal system-action layer.
- **Unknown:** which actions can be invoked and verified on target macOS with current user settings.
- **Action:** record mechanism per action, preconditions, permission needs and tested result.
- **Status:** Researching.

### Risk R-06 - Permission/signing stability

- **Known lead:** Accessibility, Input Monitoring and Automation have different flows; code signing changes can alter prompts and grants.
- **Verified preparation:** stable bundle ID, local ad-hoc Debug/Release signatures and launch; no team/usable certificate configured. Release hardened runtime has no entitlements. Xcode test preparation injects temporary Debug test-runner allowances; these are absent from final Release. [pqrs signing note](https://github.com/pqrs-org/osx-event-observer-examples/blob/c55d9c994a2aa8528e5410d5409e2a6242801e77/cgeventtap-example/README.md) reinforces identity stability for repeated builds.
- **Unknown:** actual TCC prompt details, trust restoration/revocation and Developer ID/notarization for this app; the user now confirms working permission setup and G603 recording. Control + Button 4 → Command+A in TextEdit is user-confirmed; broader output, suppression/release and lifecycle remain unverified.
- **Action:** keep bundle ID stable, test real prompts/state, record code signing settings without weakening security.
- **Status:** Researching.

## Dependency decision register

Reviewed upstream manifests, READMEs/licenses and relevant capture/source files. Maintenance dates below are reviewed default-branch commit dates unless marked as inspection-only; none guarantees quality or OS support. **No candidate runtime code was copied or installed, and no third-party library was built.** Source compatibility is declared upstream; Swift 6/macOS 26 runtime behavior still needs validation.

| Candidate / source | Purpose, compatibility and maintenance | License / limits / decision |
|---|---|---|
| [Hammerspoon screen lock](https://github.com/Hammerspoon/hammerspoon/blob/23e387e2805a9890066366e0ac96c71b27f0cfd5/extensions/caffeinate/libcaffeinate.m) | Private lock primitive/void ABI reference; non-archived, pinned commit 2026-07-08; full MIT license and source read. Broad Objective-C/Lua app, exact current app OS floor/local build unverified | MIT notice already bundled, extended reference credit. Adopt the isolated native entry point, not the app/package/source; private API may change. No Lua or binary dependency. |
| [macos-lock](https://github.com/sindresorhus/macos-lock/tree/de0756db39f3035b373a37420764fd1299dccee6) | Minimal C CLI using the same void entry point; latest pinned commit 2024-05-02, not archived. Full MIT license/source/build recipe read; no declared OS/Swift version or modern compatibility test | MIT; reference only, no executable built/run/copied. Update this table when a package is actually installed or rejected. Don't present candidate names as installed dependencies.

## Capability verification matrix

Update statuses as functionality is implemented and tested. Fill exact device + OS build + evidence.

| Capability | Initial status | Hardware / OS tested | Evidence / limitations |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keyboard shortcut capture and remap | Implemented | No physical remap test; UI inspected on macOS 27.0.1 | Category-wide ordinary keys, exact primary modifiers, one-shot output; real capture/injection requires grants and hardware |
| Physical key remap including Caps Lock | Partial implementation; Caps Lock Researching | None | Ordinary key-code trigger/output path exists; modifier-only, Caps Lock and Fn deferred; Fn-flagged events pass through |
| Middle/extra mouse recording | User-verified subset | G603 LIGHTSPEED, G HUB installed, 2026-10-08; host OS previously inventoried as 27.0.1 | User reports middle Button 3 and sides Buttons 4/5; Quartz indexes 2/3/4 follow the existing UI conversion, not an independent HID measurement. No per-device attribution proof |
| Modifier + Mouse Button 4/5 | User-verified subset | G603 LIGHTSPEED + G HUB, TextEdit, 2026-10-08; host previously inventoried as macOS 27.0.1 | User confirms Control + Button 4 → Command+A and plain Button 4 unchanged. Button 5 output, broader modifiers and lifecycle remain pending |
| Buttons beyond 5 | Implemented | None | Numeric UInt32 indexes; pure tests include high indices; actual device/driver events still pending |
| Modifier + wheel | Implemented subset | None; Russian selector/hints inspected on macOS 27.0.1 | Discrete single-axis four directions → shortcut, exact modifiers, shared 100 ms throttle; smooth/momentum/diagonal pass through; no physical attribution or real delivery result |

## Session log

### 2026-10-10 — Click-to-tap recovery after idle native frame gaps

- User reports slightly improved initial four-finger tap detection after the responsiveness patch, then no Command-W output after physical four-finger clicks. This is a new recovery failure; the previous output happy path does not accept T1/T2. Asked for the main-window engine status and whether toggling rules restores capture; no answer at verification time. The actual failing hardware sequence was not recorded, so do not claim a confirmed physical cause.
- Scope: Phase 5 / SPEC §3.4, T1/T2. Reviewed the classifier, click gate, native buffer, runtime activity cancellation, capture loop and targeted worker. Applied installed Swift Testing Pro / Swift Concurrency Pro. Re-read pinned [Sanne MIT recognizer](https://github.com/anandghegde/sanne/blob/53bee5b36ebae96a8b1228221e878de7acf79149/Sources/Sanne/Gestures/Recognizers/ThreeFingerDragRecognizer.swift): finishing/reset returns to idle rather than carrying an old contact sequence. Also inspected pinned Subsurface's ordered frame callback/stop path locally. Neither documents a promise that native IDs are consecutive across idle periods. No new dependency, copied third-party code or private ABI changes.
- Reproduced a cancellation loop in pure tests: known release, click down/up (including late mouse-up plus buffered pre-up release), then three otherwise valid taps with forward ID gaps between strokes. The old code rejects each first contact at the gap, waits for neutral, and repeats the rejection on the next tap. Two new regression functions fail with nine expectations before the fix (`/tmp/rebind-trackpad-click-recovery-before.log`, 32 tests / 2 suites). An initial method-level Xcode filter selected zero tests; reran with suite filters before drawing any conclusion.
- Allow a forward ID gap only when no stroke is open and the recognizer has observed neutral. While a qualified release awaits its click check, the session requires continuous frames; the existing pending-gap cancellation regression still passes. Active stroke gaps, stale/malformed input, unknown neutral, counter changes, duplicate/backwards IDs and nonmonotonic time still cancel. New contacts cancel previous pending output before starting a fresh stroke. No automatic action retry, device restart, engine resume, pressure-based click classification, changed latency or settings/rule writes.
- Added three test functions covering repeated idle-separated taps, click → neutral → multiple tap recovery for both button ordering cases, and backwards/duplicate idle IDs that reject but recover after fresh neutral. Software source/sequence evidence is verified; whether idle gaps caused the user's actual failure and whether the physical device recovers require a retest.
- Verification: `scripts/dev.sh test -resultBundlePath build/TrackpadClickRecoveryFinalTests.xcresult`: **479 tests / 63 suites / 0 failures**, `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-click-recovery-tests-final.log`. `scripts/dev.sh build` and `scripts/dev.sh release`: **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-click-recovery-debug.log` and `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-click-recovery-release.log`. Universal Release (`x86_64 arm64`) passes strict/deep codesign verification. No Swift warnings; the existing unused AppIntents metadata extraction warning/test-host linkd messages remain non-failing. `git diff --check` passes. Native hardware and pending engine-status reply remain separate; no real input posting/capture, TCC grants, UI/Sketch edits or commits. Existing user/brand work preserved.
- **Next:** after relaunching the rebuilt copy, alternate light four-finger taps and quick/held physical clicks in disposable tabs. Physical clicks must not send Command-W; subsequent taps should work without another relaunch or rule toggle. If they fail again, record the main-window engine/trackpad status and whether toggle/resume restores it. Revised usability, exclusivity, sleep/wake/stop/recovery and system conflicts remain open.


### 2026-10-10 — Four-finger tap responsiveness and natural-contact tolerances

- User confirms the integrated app sends the configured shortcut, but only about one tap in seven is recognized and output feels delayed versus BetterTouchTool. Record this as a usability failure; output happy path does not close T1/T2 or click/lifecycle gates. Scope: Phase 5 / SPEC §3.4. Applied Swift Concurrency Pro, Swift Testing Pro and Writing for Interfaces; existing layout/brand changes preserved.
- Re-read pinned [Sanne ThreeFingerDragRecognizer](https://github.com/anandghegde/sanne/blob/53bee5b36ebae96a8b1228221e878de7acf79149/Sources/Sanne/Gestures/Recognizers/ThreeFingerDragRecognizer.swift), GestureEngine and TouchDeviceManager. Sanne uses a 400 ms tap limit and normalized centroid travel 0.028, completes on falling contact count and uses local receipt time even for empty arrays. Existing research record: MIT, Swift tools 5.9 / macOS 13, no package dependencies, latest reviewed commit 2026-10-05; full app, not an embeddable strict-callback engine. Its logs/locks/drag output and centroid-only classification are not imported. Rebind keeps per-contact identity/travel to reject opposing-finger movement. No third-party code copied or new dependency/helper added.
- Reviewed [BetterTouchTool’s official trackpad trigger documentation](https://docs.folivora.ai/docs/trackpad-mouse/magic-mouse-trackpad/): tap/click presets and configuration are documented, but the page does not supply a reusable recognizer or its internal latency algorithm. Do not claim BTT-equivalent detection from documentation. Native callback ABI remains the pinned Apache-2.0 Subsurface signature (Double timestamp / Int32 frame), inspected in the resolved package sources; existing bundled notice applies.
- Root causes found in code: 500 ms intentional settling; strict 80 ms finger arrival/lift, 250 ms total duration, 0.02 individual travel and 15 ms peak dwell; callback-level native time/frame discarded, leaving zero-record releases unable to finish a stroke. Forward callback metadata for every native frame, including nil/zero arrays, through the bounded ring. Keep record-header consistency, malformed metadata, stale frames, monotonic/consecutive callback sequence and overflow guards. A release without timing still cannot qualify. Real timing/ABI behavior needs the new hardware retest.
- Revised natural-contact bounds: 30–400 ms total duration, 150 ms arrival/lift spread, 0.04 individual travel, two peak frames (including an 8 ms interval). Preserve identity replacement/bounce, five-contact, hold/movement and >50 ms active-frame-gap rejection. Settle for 60 ms after final lift; the recorded native +12/+28 ms button pair fits that interval, but arbitrary click latency/source is not proven. Key/button/wheel/new-contact/focus/runtime cancellation and held-state/target/expiry checks remain. No pressure-only click classifier, native suppression or system-preference changes.
- Added 11 behavioral Swift Testing functions: fast peak, 320 ms rolling contacts with 3% drift, opposing movement/slow-lift negatives, callback-clock/empty-release validation, repeated taps without half-second dead time and delayed click cancellation at +12/+28/+59 ms. Updated old boundary negatives and timing expectations. Tests use injected frames/clocks/senders, no real capture/output, permissions or user-rule writes.
- Updated the EN/RU/DE/FR hint to describe output after lift/click exclusion without an obsolete half-second promise. SPEC, README and the hardware walkthrough record current bounds, user feedback and remaining checks. No UI layout redesign or Sketch edits.
- Verification: `scripts/dev.sh test -resultBundlePath build/TrackpadResponsivenessTests.xcresult`: **476 tests / 63 suites / 0 failures**, `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-responsive-tests.log`. `scripts/dev.sh build` and `scripts/dev.sh release`: **BUILD SUCCEEDED**, `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-responsive-debug.log` and `/tmp/rebind-trackpad-responsive-release.log`. Universal Release (`x86_64 arm64`) passes `codesign --verify --deep --strict`. No new Swift/compiler warnings; test-target AppIntents metadata extraction warnings and test-host linkd autoShortcut connection messages do not fail the suite and do not exercise trackpad delivery. String Catalog parses with all four hint translations; `git diff --check` passes. No commits.
- **Next:** user retests 10–20 natural four-finger taps in disposable tabs, quick physical clicks, modest rolling placement/movement, repeat taps and pause/profile/focus cancellation. Report recognition frequency and perceived delay. Revised usability, actual end-to-end latency, click exclusivity, stop/recovery and system conflicts remain unverified; T1/T2 stay open.

### 2026-10-09 — Qualified trackpad tap sequences and MIT corner reuse

- Continued Phase 5 / SPEC §3.4, T1/T2 after the alternatives review. Applied Swift Concurrency Pro and Swift Testing Pro. Single taps are restricted to 3/4; doubles to 2/3/4; one-finger singles are four configured corners only. A single is immediate unless a matching double is enabled. For competing single/double rules the second contact must start before the first release + interval; a valid second contact may finish later without a premature first single. Doubles deliver once and do not reuse the second tap. Mismatched counts/centroid locations end a pair; no unsupported trigger/output is produced.
- The API deliberately accepts only movement/continuity-validated lifted contacts with **independently established click exclusion**. `clickExcluded=false` cancels the whole pair; absence of an observed Quartz button is not asserted to establish exclusion. `cancel()` must be called by the future adapter on buttons, uncertain frames, motion, pause/profile changes, permission/session loss and sleep/disconnect. These production hooks are **not wired**. Lost releases, duplicate starts, backwards clocks, invalid positions/counts/durations and deadline overflow clear state. ### 2026-10-09 — Application-switch failure and activation-notification order

- User reports application switching does not work, without app/error details. Read-only inspection of the saved selected profile identifies an enabled Previous Application rule on Quartz button 4 (UI button 5), with global rules enabled; did not change configuration, permissions or launch a second engine.
- Apple [notification documentation](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appkit/nsworkspace/didactivateapplicationnotification) / DocC JSON specify the affected app in `applicationUserInfoKey`; SDK 27 `NSRunningApplication.h` documents cached changing properties until the next main run-loop turn. The old selector ignored that payload and resampled foreground. A single delayed notification was already reconciled correctly; multiple activations before the cached foreground advances can lose the intermediate app and select the wrong previous target.
- Receive the actual notification argument and record its eligible affected app. Sample foreground only at start/dispatch. Keep two handles, MainActor ownership, stop cleanup, Rebind/nonregular exclusions, the reentrancy-safe snapshot and all final guards. Reuse the existing client protocol as a test seam for the actual NotificationCenter/Objective-C selector bridge; no runtime dependency or new dispatch queue.
- Four new Swift Testing functions exercise cached-property notification bursts, duplicate/own/ineligible/malformed events, stop/restart and reentrant activation. Temporarily restoring the old handler makes the burst test choose `[1, 3]` instead of `[2, 3]`; both assertions fail. Xcode then stalled while finalizing that deliberately failed run; stopped only owned xcodebuild processes and restored the fixed source. An overlapping final attempt was cancelled and is not counted as verification. The first simple two-app regression passed with the old handler, so it is not evidence of this bug.
- Applied Swift Concurrency Pro / Swift Testing Pro. Fresh Apple cooperative-activation docs and Hammerspoon native activation source were inspected: acceptance still does not guarantee focus; Hammerspoon's force/deprecated paths were not adopted. No claim that this ordering defect explains every reported failure, or that native activation is now hardware-verified.
- Final verification: `scripts/dev.sh test -resultBundlePath build/ApplicationSwitchObserver-Final2.xcresult` passes **411 functions in 57 suites**; `/tmp/rebind-app-switch-fix-final2-tests.log`. Ordinary `scripts/dev.sh build` after tests and `scripts/dev.sh release` pass; `/tmp/rebind-app-switch-fix-final-build.log` / `/tmp/rebind-app-switch-fix-final-release.log`. Debug/Release strict signature checks pass; Release contains arm64/x86_64. Only the existing no-AppIntents metadata warning and test-host linkd connection diagnostics remain; no Swift compile errors. `git diff --check` passes. Real Finder/TextEdit toggle and focus check remain required; if it still fails, capture the UI error and exact app pair to separate unavailable history from native activation refusal. No commits.

### 2026-10-08 — Public AX active-window/application action slice

- Continued Phase 4 / SPEC §5 A1/A2 with four real actions in the shared catalog: close/minimize active window, press its public full-screen button and hide active application. Required support is checked on the eventual target; the editor explains the consumed-input/error contract. No key/AppleScript fallback or private `AXFullScreen` attribute.
- Public ApplicationServices adapter stays on the bounded serial action worker. Require AX trust, external PID/ownership, window role, enabled button or writable property; recheck focus and dispatch generation/permissions/Secure Input before mutation. Hide works without a window; Rebind itself is excluded. IPC uses ≤150 ms request timeouts and a 450 ms budget for starting requests; these are OS request limits, not measured worst-case performance. AX acceptance is not completion; final focus/call race remains non-atomic. No titles/text/contents/positions read or logged.

### 2026-10-08 — Receiver unplug confirmation and experimental mouse sender probe

- User confirms the inventory works and the receiver disappears when disconnected (G603 LIGHTSPEED, G HUB setup). Mark only this removal happy path verified; reconnect, individual button metadata, source identity and identifier stability remain open.
- Scope: Phase 3, SPEC §§3.2–3.3, 6–7, 9, 12, D1/D2 and S1/S2/S3. Research rechecked Apple CGEvent/source-process documentation, current LinearMouse device routing and its bridging-header ABI declarations, MIT license, plus GitHub and Swift Package Index candidates. Public source process/state fields are not physical HID identity. CoreHID can enumerate and observe a device but has no documented join key to the suppressible Quartz event. No reliable public per-event routing package was established by these searches; this is not proof that none exists. Several guessed header/pinned URLs failed; direct shell GitHub API download failed DNS. The accessible primary pages provided the findings below.
- **Decision:** use a small original, isolated `MouseEventSourceProbe`, dynamically resolving `CGEventCopyIOHIDEvent` / `IOHIDEventGetSenderID`. LinearMouse is a maintained MIT reference app (previous pinned audit plus current main inspected); do not import its full app, copied routing/fallbacks or new package. [Current routing](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LinearMouse/Device/DeviceManager.swift), [ABI declarations](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LinearMouse/LinearMouse-Bridging-Header.h), [MIT license](https://github.com/linearmouse/linearmouse/blob/main/LICENSE), [Apple CGEvent](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgevent), [source process ID](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coregraphics/cgeventfield/eventsourceunixprocessid). Private API has no supported macOS/Swift compatibility guarantee; introduced only for this explicitly experimental measurement because the public tap has no documented mouse identity. No helper, root, driver, new grants or SPM dependency.
- The probe is worker-owned, loads symbols between run-loop iterations and balances Copy ownership. Only separately enabled, quota-eligible button/drag/wheel diagnostics copy an underlying event; keyboard, idle motion, tagged Rebind feedback, Secure Input, stopped/suspended/expired/rate-limited sessions skip lookup. Raw UInt64 sender values never enter matching keys, device scopes or persistence. Missing symbols/events and sender 0 show Unknown; no last-active/time-proximity guess. Derived hold/motion rows have no inferred ID. Protected/capture loss clears source data/mode. Existing ring/rate/deadline bounds remain in force; actual callback latency remains a hardware gate.
- Native metadata-only `/tmp/rebind-source-symbol-probe` compiled with Swift 6: both symbols resolve on the current host; a constructed mouse event returns unavailable. This tests availability/negative behavior only, not a real source or macOS 26 ABI.
- `scripts/dev.sh doctor`: Xcode 27.0 / Swift 6.4 / SDK 27.0, Apple Silicon macOS 27.0.1 (26A434), deployment 26 unchanged. `scripts/dev.sh test -resultBundlePath build/Phase3MouseSourceTests-Final.xcresult`: **180 tests / 27 suites / 0 failures**, eight new functions covering separate opt-in, bounded extraction budget, source unknowns, no keyboard/feedback rows, category-rule invariance and release symmetry, protected clearing, unsupported scroll/derived hold metadata and constructed-event adapter behavior. Found NSTableView retaining its old conditional-column layout; table identity now changes only when its column set changes, preserving normal row-refresh identity. Existing three mouse rules remain loaded/paused; no saves/toggles/TCC grants. Inspection copy lacks actual AX/post permissions, so no real source captured by the agent. - Unified capture-loss reset for missing permissions, Secure Input, suspension, stop, tap install/interruption and output failure. Active recorder receives a matching cancellation result instead of remaining stuck; pending actions, holds, pointer capture and source ownership are discarded. Interruption/output failure pauses and requires explicit Resume/Record retry; permission restoration keeps the user's enable preference and starts a fresh acquisition.
- Worker also checks live tap validity/enabled state before queued dispatch so a lost callback stream is not considered healthy. A latched failure stays visible during/after Secure Input rather than leaving a stale protected-input status. Port remains on its original thread; no callback Tasks/actor hops/UI/posting/I/O/logging. Real port-disable detection and latency remain unverified.
- Track sleep and inactive user session separately; waking one does not resume the other. Duplicate/unrelated notifications do not clear the remaining suspension. UI recorder cancels on suspension/termination; permission refresh is requested on the next UI timer tick. NSWorkspace session notifications do not establish a general screen-lock detector.
- `scripts/dev.sh doctor`: Xcode 27.0 / Swift 6.4 / SDK 27.0, Apple Silicon macOS 27.0.1 (26A434), deployment remains 26. `scripts/dev.sh test -resultBundlePath build/Phase2LifecycleTests-Final.xcresult`: **143 tests / 22 suites / 0 failures**, 12 new test functions (two parameterized) plus all previous 131. Tests use local models/mailboxes and explicit notification handlers; no real system sleep, tap, global output, permission changes or user storage.
- Final `scripts/dev.sh build` / `scripts/dev.sh release`: **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. Release arm64/x86_64, `codesign --verify --deep --strict --verbose=2` and packaged privacy-manifest lint pass; no Intel/macOS 26 execution claim. Expected AppIntents metadata-skip and hosted autoShortcut diagnostics; no source/concurrency warnings. `git diff --check` passes.
